Inequalities in homogeneous Triebel-Lizorkin and Besov-Lipschitz spaces
Lifeng Wang

Abstract
. This paper provides equivalence characterizations of homogeneous Triebel-Lizorkin and Besov-Lipschitz spaces, denoted by ​ F ˙ p , q s ​ ( ℝ n ) \dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n}) and B ˙ p , q s ​ ( ℝ n ) \dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n}) respectively, in terms of maximal functions of the mean values of iterated difference. It also furnishes the reader with inequalities in F ˙ p , q s ​ ( ℝ n ) \dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n}) in terms of iterated difference and in terms of iterated difference along coordinate axes. The corresponding inequalities in B ˙ p , q s ​ ( ℝ n ) \dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n}) in terms of iterated difference and in terms of iterated difference along coordinate axes are also considered. The techniques used in this paper are of Fourier analytic nature and the Hardy-Littlewood and Peetre-Fefferman-Stein maximal functions.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
42B35.

1.
Introduction

We begin by introducing notations, definitions, historical results, and the main theorems of this paper. Let
\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
denote the space of Schwartz functions on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
be the space of tempered distributions on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For a function
f\in L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, we denote its
n
-dimensional Fourier transform by

\mathcal{F}_{n}f(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f(x)e^{-2\pi ix\cdot\xi}dx,

and the
n
-dimensional inverse Fourier transform is denoted by

\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}f(\xi)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f(x)e^{2\pi ix\cdot\xi}dx,

where for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
x\cdot\xi
is the inner product. If
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, we use the same notation to denote the
n
-dimensional distributional Fourier transform and its inverse. Now we introduce the definitions of related function spaces and maximal functions. We fix throughout this paper
\phi,\psi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
such that

0\leq\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(\xi)\leq 1\qquad\text{and}\qquad spt.\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi\subseteq\{\frac{1}{2}\leq|\xi|<2\}

(1)

and also

\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{-j}\xi)=1\qquad\text{if}\qquad\xi\neq 0,

(2)

then the function
\phi
is defined in a way so that

\mathcal{F}_{n}\phi(\xi)=\begin{cases}\sum_{j\leq 0}\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{-j}\xi)&\text{ if }\xi\neq 0,\\
1&\text{ if }\xi=0.\end{cases}

(3)

then

spt.\mathcal{F}_{n}\phi(\xi)\subseteq\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|\xi|\leq 2\}\text{ and }\mathcal{F}_{n}\phi(\xi)=1\text{ if }|\xi|\leq 1,

(4)

furthermore we have the equality

\mathcal{F}_{n}\phi(\xi)+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{-j}\xi)=1\text{ for all }\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(5)

Define for
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, the function
f_{j}(x):=\psi_{2^{-j}}*f(x)
where
\psi_{2^{-j}}(y)=2^{jn}\psi(2^{j}y)
and thus we have the following decompositions:

f=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}f_{j},

(6)

where the sum in (
6
) converges in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})/\mathscr{P}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})/\mathscr{P}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is the space of tempered distributions modulo polynomials (cf.
[
3
, section 1.1.1]
), and

f=f*\phi+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}f_{j},

(7)

where the sum in (
7
) converges in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Also due to the support condition of
\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi
, we have the following

f_{j}(x)=\sum_{l=j-1}^{j+1}(f_{j})_{l}(x)\qquad\text{for almost every }x\in\mathbb{R}^{n},

(8)

where
(f_{j})_{l}=\psi_{2^{-l}}*f_{j}=\psi_{2^{-l}}*\psi_{2^{-j}}*f
. Given a sequence
\{f_{k}(x)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
of functions defined on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
0<p,q\leq\infty
, we use the notation
\|\{f_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}
to denote the
\|\cdot\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
-quasinorm of the
\|\cdot\|_{l^{q}}
-quasinorm of the sequence
\{f_{k}(x)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
and the notation
\|\{f_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})}
represents the
\|\cdot\|_{l^{q}}
-quasinorm of the sequence

\{\|f_{k}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
. Furthermore,
\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f_{k}(x)|
denotes the essential supremum of the function
|f_{k}(x)|
over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, that is, the least upper bound of
|f_{k}(x)|
over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
except on a subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
of Lebesgue measure zero. Moreover,
\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}|f_{k}(x)|
denotes the essential supremum of the sequence
\{|f_{k}(x)|\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
at
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, that is, the least upper bound of
\{|f_{k}(x)|\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
except on a subset of
\mathbb{Z}
of counting measure zero, and in this sense
\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}|f_{k}(x)|=\sup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}|f_{k}(x)|
.

Definition 1.1
.

For
0<p<\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, the homogeneous Triebel-Lizorkin space
\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
as a subspace of the space
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})/\mathscr{P}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is

\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})=\{f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})/\mathscr{P}(\mathbb{R}^{n}):\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}:=\|\{2^{ks}f_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}<\infty\}.

(9)

For
0<p\leq\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, the homogeneous Besov-Lipschitz space
\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
as a subspace of the space
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})/\mathscr{P}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is

\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\!=\!\{f\!\in\!\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})/\mathscr{P}(\mathbb{R}^{n}):\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\!:=\!\|\{2^{ks}f_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})}\!<\!\infty\}.

(10)

It is a well-known fact that the space
\mathcal{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
of Schwartz functions that satisfy the condition

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}x^{\alpha}\varphi(x)dx=0\text{ for all multi-indices }\alpha

is dense in
\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
when
0<p,q<\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, and this condition is also equivalent to the condition that all the derivatives of the Fourier transform
\mathcal{F}_{n}\varphi
equal to
0
at the origin.

Definition 1.2
.

For
0<p<\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, the inhomogeneous Triebel-Lizorkin space
F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
as a subspace of the space
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is

F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\!=\!\{f\!\in\!\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\!:\!\|f\|_{F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\!:=\!\|\phi*\!f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\!+\!\|\{2^{ks}\!f_{k}\}_{k>0}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}\!\!<\!\!\infty\}.

(11)

For
0<p\leq\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, the inhomogeneous Besov-Lipschitz space
B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
as a subspace of the space
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
is

B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\!=\!\{f\!\in\!\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\!:\!\|f\|_{B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\!\!:=\!\!\|\phi*\!f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\!+\!\|\{2^{ks}\!f_{k}\}_{k>0}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})}\!\!<\!\!\infty\}.

(12)

Here we give the definition of iterated difference. Let
\mathbb{N}
denote the set of positive integers and
L\in\mathbb{N}
. For a function
f
defined on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
x,h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
we define

\varDelta^{1}_{h}f(x)=f(x+h)-f(x),\qquad(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f)(x)=\varDelta^{1}_{h}(\varDelta^{L-1}_{h}f)(x).

(13)

It is not hard to prove by induction on
L
that

(-1)^{L+1}(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f)(x)=\sum^{L}_{j=1}d_{j}f(x+jh)-f(x),

(14)

where

\sum^{L}_{j=1}d_{j}=1\text{ and }d_{j}\in\mathbb{Z}\text{ for }1\leq j\leq L.

(15)

When
f
is in
\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, we have

(\varDelta^{1}_{h}\mathcal{F}_{n}f)(x)=\mathcal{F}_{n}((e^{-2\pi ih\cdot\xi}-1)f(\xi))(x),

(16)

and by iteration, we can obtain

(\varDelta^{L}_{h}\mathcal{F}_{n}f)(x)=\mathcal{F}_{n}((e^{-2\pi ih\cdot\xi}-1)^{L}f(\xi))(x).

(17)

In a similar way, we also have

(\varDelta^{L}_{h}\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}f)(x)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}((e^{2\pi ih\cdot\xi}-1)^{L}f(\xi))(x),

(18)

and therefore the following is true

(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f)(x)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}((e^{2\pi ih\cdot\xi}-1)^{L}\mathcal{F}_{n}f(\xi))(x).

(19)

Given
L\in\mathbb{N}
,
h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, observe the facts that
<\varDelta^{L}_{h}f,\varphi>=<f,\varDelta^{L}_{-h}\varphi>
and both spaces
\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\mathcal{S}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
are closed under the operation
\varDelta^{L}_{-h}
, then (
6
) and (
7
) also suggest that

\varDelta^{L}_{h}f=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j}\qquad\text{in the sense of }\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})/\mathscr{P}(\mathbb{R}^{n}),

(20)

and

\varDelta^{L}_{h}f=\varDelta^{L}_{h}(f*\phi)+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j}\qquad\text{in the sense of }\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n}).

(21)

We introduce some maximal functions below. Let ​
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}
​ denote the ​
(n\!-\!1)
-dimensional Hausdorff measure on the unit sphere
\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
A
denote the annulus
A:=\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:1\leq|z|<2\}
. In this paper we denote the Lebesgue measures and integrals with respect to Lebesgue measures in the usual way, then
|\mathbb{S}^{n-1}|=\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\mathbb{S}^{n-1})
and
|A|
represent the corresponding surface measure and volume respectively. Also “
\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0pt}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits}
” is the mean value integral.

Definition 1.3
.

For a function
f
defined on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, let
t>0
,
r>0
,
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
0\neq h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
L\in\mathbb{N}
, then the following maximal functions are defined

\displaystyle(S^{L}_{t}f)(x)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0pt\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}(\varDelta^{L}_{tz}f)(x-y)d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(z)|\cdot(1+t^{-1}|y|)^{\frac{-n}{r}},

(22)

\displaystyle(V^{L}_{t}f)(x)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0ptA}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A}}(\varDelta^{L}_{tz}f)(x-y)dz|\cdot(1+t^{-1}|y|)^{\frac{-n}{r}},

(23)

\displaystyle(D^{L}_{h}f)(x)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f)(x-y)|\cdot(1+\frac{|y|}{|h|})^{\frac{-n}{r}}.

(24)

Now we propose the first pair of equivalence characterizations for homogeneous
\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
spaces. We recall in the theory of tempered distributions, the integral of an equivalence class is justified when the equivalence class has a function representative and integrals of distinct representatives in the same equivalence class are identified. The same principle also applies to the supremum of an equivalence class. Integrals and supremums included in the main theorems and their corollaries below are all considered this way.

Theorem 1.4
.

Let
n\geq 2
,
0<p<\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
,
f\in\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
has a function representative and assume
L\in\mathbb{N}
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
satisfy
\frac{n}{\min\{p,q\}}<s<L
, then for every
r
as in Definition
1.3
satisfying
\frac{n}{s}<r<\min\{p,q\}
, the following five quasinorms are equivalent quasinorms in
\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,

\displaystyle\|\{2^{ks}S^{L}_{2^{-k}}f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})},

(25)

\displaystyle\|\{2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{0<\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})},

(26)

\displaystyle\|\{2^{ks}V^{L}_{2^{-k}}f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})},

(27)

\displaystyle\|\{2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{0<\tau<2}V^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})},

(28)

\displaystyle\|\{2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{0<|h|<2}D^{L}_{2^{-k}h}f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}.

(29)

Theorem 1.5
.

Let
n\geq 2
,
0<p\leq\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
,
f\in\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
has a function representative and assume
L\in\mathbb{N}
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
satisfy
\frac{n}{p}<s<L
, then for every
r
as in Definition
1.3
satisfying
\frac{n}{s}<r<p
, the following five quasinorms are equivalent quasinorms in
\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,

\displaystyle\|\{2^{ks}S^{L}_{2^{-k}}f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})},

(30)

\displaystyle\|\{2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{0<\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})},

(31)

\displaystyle\|\{2^{ks}V^{L}_{2^{-k}}f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})},

(32)

\displaystyle\|\{2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{0<\tau<2}V^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})},

(33)

\displaystyle\|\{2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{0<|h|<2}D^{L}_{2^{-k}h}f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})}.

(34)

Equivalence characterizations of the same type for inhomogeneous Triebel-Lizorkin and Besov-Lipschitz spaces were obtained by H. Triebel in
[
7
, section 2.5.9]
with unnatural restrictions
s\geq G_{p}
,
M>2G_{p}+s
,
s\geq G_{pq}
,
M>2G_{pq}+s
where
G_{p}=n+3+\frac{3n}{p}
and
G_{pq}=n+3+\frac{3n}{\min\{p,q\}}
. Theorems
1.4
and
1.5
are both new and present optimal conditions on
s
and
L
and extend the ranges of
\tau
and
|h|
under the supremums in H. Triebel’s result. The proof of Theorem
1.4
can be found in section
3
and the proof of Theorem
1.5
is given in section
4
.

This paper also furnishes the reader with a theorem of inequalities in the homogeneous Triebel-Lizorkin space
\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
in terms of iterated difference, which includes the case
0<q<1
. The theorem was proven by using Fourier analytic techniques and we now state this result below. Let

\sigma_{pq}=\max\{0,n(\frac{1}{\min\{p,q\}}-1)\},\qquad\tilde{\sigma}_{pq}=\max\{0,n(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q})\}.

(35)

Theorem 1.6
.

Let
L\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<p<\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
f\in\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

(i)If
0<p,q<\infty
,
\tilde{\sigma}_{pq}<s<L
, then

\|(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-sq}|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f|^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(36)

(ii)Suppose
f
has a function representative. If
0<p<\infty
,
0<q<1
and
\sigma_{pq}+\tilde{\sigma}_{pq}<s<\infty
, or if
0<p<\infty
,
1\leq q<\infty
and
-n<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-sq}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f)(\cdot)|^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(37)

(iii)If
0<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
\frac{n}{p}<s<L
, then

\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f|}{|h|^{s}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(38)

(iv)Suppose
f
has a function representative. If
0<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
-n<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f)(\cdot)|}{|h|^{s}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(39)

The proof of Theorem
1.6
can be found in section
5
. We believe Theorem
1.6
(ii) and (iv) are new and better than existing results. Theorem
1.6
(i) shows the term

\|(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-sq}|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f|^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

(40)

may not be independently defined for tempered distributions, since the iterated difference
\varDelta^{L}_{h}f
may not have a function representative if
f
is a member of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Another example is that if
P(x)=x^{\alpha}
is a polynomial function and we put it into (
40
) then the resulting term may not have a finite value. However if we consider
P
as a tempered distribution in
\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and the conditions of Theorem
1.6
(i) are met, then (
255
) designates the function representative of
\varDelta^{L}_{h}P
is given by
\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\varDelta^{L}_{h}(\psi_{2^{-j}}*P)(x)=0
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. This is because
(\psi_{2^{-j}}*P)(x)
can be expressed as a linear combination, with coefficients depending on
x
, of derivatives of the Fourier transform
\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi
evaluated at
0
and these evaluations are identically zero due to the support condition of
\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi
. We believe Theorem
1.6
(i) extends the definition of the term (
40
). The same discussion is also true for Theorem
1.6
(iii).

In
[
7
, section 2.5.10]
, H. Triebel gave an equivalence characterization theorem of the inhomogeneous Triebel-Lizorkin space
F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
in terms of iterated difference, where the conditions for the parameters are
0<p<\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
and
\frac{n}{\min\{p,q\}}<s<M
. In
[
6
, Theorem 1 pp. 102]
, E. M. Stein gave the equivalence characterization

[f]_{W^{\alpha}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\sim\|f\|_{L^{p}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

where the restrictions
0<\alpha<1
,
1<p<\infty
and
\frac{2n}{n+2\alpha}<p<\infty
were considered essentially sharp. And for
1<p<\infty
,
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
and
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, the inhomogeneous Sobolev norm of
f
(cf.
[
3
, section 1.3.1]
) is defined to be

\|f\|_{L_{\alpha}^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}:=\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}((1+|\xi|^{2})^{\alpha/2}\mathcal{F}_{n}f)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

(41)

where
\|\cdot\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
is considered via duality. Since the inhomogeneous Triebel-Lizorkin space satisfies
L^{p}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\sim F^{\alpha}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
if
1<p<\infty
and since

[f]_{W^{\alpha}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=\|(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-2\alpha}|(\varDelta^{1}_{h}f)(\cdot)|^{2}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n}})^{\frac{1}{2}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

we consider E. M. Stein’s result is an improvement of H. Triebel’s. Furthermore in
[
5
, Theorem 1 pp. 393]
, A. Seeger provided an equivalence characterization for the homogeneous anisotropic space

\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{\alpha}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\sim\|S^{\alpha}_{q,r,m}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

where
0<p<\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
,
m>\alpha/a_{0}
,
r\geq 1
with

\alpha>\max\{0,\nu(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{r}),\nu(\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{r})\},

and

S^{\alpha}_{q,r,m}f(x)=(\int_{0}^{\infty}[\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0pt\varrho(h)\leq t}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\varrho(h)\leq t}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\varrho(h)\leq t}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\varrho(h)\leq t}}|(\varDelta^{m}_{h}f)(x)|^{r}dh]^{q/r}\frac{dt}{t^{1+\alpha q}})^{1/q}.

If we consider the isotropic case of A. Seeger’s result, in which
\varrho(h)
above can be deemed as
|h|
and
a_{0}
can be deemed as
1
, then by letting
r=q
and changing the order of integration we can obtain

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{\alpha}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\sim\|(\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{|h|\leq t}t^{-1-n-q\alpha}\cdot|(\varDelta^{m}_{h}f)(\cdot)|^{q}dhdt)^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\sim\|(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-q\alpha}\cdot|(\varDelta^{m}_{h}f)(\cdot)|^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

for
0<p<\infty
,
1\leq q\leq\infty
and
\max\{0,\nu(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q})\}<\alpha<m
. Recently in
[
4
, Theorem 1.2 pp. 693]
M. Prats proves an equivalence characterization theorem of the inhomogeneous norm
\|f\|_{F^{s}_{p,q}(\Omega)}
in terms of the sum of
\|f\|_{W^{k,p}(\Omega)}
and

\sum_{|\alpha|=k}\big(\int_{\Omega}\big(\int_{\Omega}\frac{|D^{\alpha}f(x)-D^{\alpha}f(y)|^{q}}{|x-y|^{\sigma q+d}}dy\big)^{\frac{p}{q}}dx\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}

(42)

when parameters satisfy
1\leq p<\infty,1\leq q\leq\infty,s=k+\sigma
,
\max\{0,d(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q})\}<\sigma<1
and
\Omega
is a uniform domain in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Furthermore, M. Prats also shows under the same conditions on parameters, the equivalence relation stands if (
42
) is replaced by

\sum_{|\alpha|=k}\big(\int_{\Omega}\big(\int_{\mathbf{Sh}(x)}\frac{|D^{\alpha}f(x)-D^{\alpha}f(y)|^{q}}{|x-y|^{\sigma q+d}}dy\big)^{\frac{p}{q}}dx\big)^{\frac{1}{p}}

(43)

where
\mathbf{Sh}(x):=\{y\in\Omega:|y-x|\leq c_{\Omega}\delta(x)\}
is the Carleson box centered at
x
,
\delta(x)={\rm dist\,}(x,\partial\Omega)
and
c_{\Omega}>1
is a constant. Moreover when
1\leq q\leq p<\infty
, the set
\mathbf{Sh}(x)
in (
43
) can be improved and replaced by the Whitney ball
B(x,\rho\delta(x))
for
0<\rho<1
.

Comparing Theorem
1.6
with H. Triebel’s result in
[
7
, section 2.5.10]
, we find that if
0<q<1
and
\frac{q}{q+1}\leq p<\infty
, then the restriction
\sigma_{pq}+\tilde{\sigma}_{pq}<s<L
is better than the restriction of
s
in H. Triebel’s result. However if
0<p<\frac{q}{q+1}<q<1
then we have

\frac{n}{\min\{p,q\}}<\sigma_{pq}+\tilde{\sigma}_{pq}

and the restrictions of H. Triebel’s result remain better. If in addition the number
s
also satisfies the condition

s\leq n+\frac{n}{q},

(44)

then
\sigma_{pq}+\tilde{\sigma}_{pq}<s
implies
\frac{q}{q+1}<p
and hence the restrictions in Theorem
1.6
are better than the restrictions in H. Triebel’s result. This happens for sure when we pick
L=1
or
L=2
since
n+\frac{n}{q}>2
for
0<q<1
. Therefore we formulate these two cases as corollaries below. When
0<p,q<\infty
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
f
is a function, we define the generalized Gagliardo seminorm of
f
(cf.
[
1
, section 2 pp. 524]
for the usual Gagliardo seminorm) is

[f]_{W^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}:=\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{q}}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dy\right)^{\frac{p}{q}}dx\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

(45)

Corollary 1.7
.

Let
0<p<\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
f\in\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
has a function representative.

(i)If
0<p,q<\infty
,
\tilde{\sigma}_{pq}<s<1
, then

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{q}}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dy)^{\frac{p}{q}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(46)

(ii)If
0<p<\infty
,
0<q<1
and
\sigma_{pq}+\tilde{\sigma}_{pq}<s<\infty
, or if
0<p<\infty
,
1\leq q<\infty
and
-n<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{q}}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dy)^{\frac{p}{q}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

(47)

(iii)If
0<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
\frac{n}{p}<s<1
, then

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{p}}{|x-y|^{sp}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(48)

(iv)If
0<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
-n<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{p}}{|x-y|^{sp}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

(49)

Proof of Corollary
1.7
.

Apply inequalities (
36
), (
37
), (
38
) and (
39
) with
L=1
and use appropriate change of variable. In the case
1\leq q\leq\infty
, inequalities (
47
) and (
49
) are still true for
s=0
or
s=1
. In particular, if we let
0<p\leq 1,q=2,s=0
and apply the equivalence relation
\|\cdot\|_{\dot{F}^{0}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\sim\|\cdot\|_{H^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
in (
47
), then we have

\|f\|_{H^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{2}}{|x-y|^{n}}dy)^{\frac{p}{2}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}},

(50)

where
\|\cdot\|_{H^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
represents the Hardy quasinorm.
∎

Corollary 1.8
.

Let
0<p<\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
f\in\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
has a function representative.

(i)If
0<p,q<\infty
,
\tilde{\sigma}_{pq}<s<2
, then

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)+f(y)-2f(\frac{x+y}{2})|^{q}}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dy)^{\frac{p}{q}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(51)

(ii)If
0<p<\infty
,
0<q<1
and
\sigma_{pq}+\tilde{\sigma}_{pq}<s<\infty
, or if
0<p<\infty
,
1\leq q<\infty
and
-n<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)+f(y)-2f(\frac{x+y}{2})|^{q}}{|x-y|^{n+sq}}dy)^{\frac{p}{q}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

(52)

(iii)If
0<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
\frac{n}{p}<s<2
, then

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)+f(y)-2f(\frac{x+y}{2})|^{p}}{|x-y|^{sp}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(53)

(iv)If
0<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
-n<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)+f(y)-2f(\frac{x+y}{2})|^{p}}{|x-y|^{sp}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

(54)

Proof of Corollary
1.8
.

Apply inequalities (
36
), (
37
), (
38
) and (
39
) with
L=2
and use appropriate change of variable. In the case
1\leq q\leq\infty
, inequalities (
52
) and (
54
) are still true for
s=0,1,2
. In particular, if we let
0<p\leq 1,q=2,s=0
and apply the equivalence relation
\|\cdot\|_{\dot{F}^{0}_{p,2}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\sim\|\cdot\|_{H^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
in (
52
), then we have

\|f\|_{H^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)+f(y)-2f(\frac{x+y}{2})|^{2}}{|x-y|^{n}}dy)^{\frac{p}{2}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}},

(55)

where
\|\cdot\|_{H^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
represents the Hardy quasinorm.
∎

In Theorem
1.6
, it was shown that the quasinorm
\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
is equivalent to

\|(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-sq}|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f|^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=\|(\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-sq}|\varDelta^{L}_{t\theta}f|^{q}\frac{dt}{t}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta))^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

and the expression inside the parenthesis on the right side is an integral of the term
\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-sq}|\varDelta^{L}_{t\theta}f|^{q}\frac{dt}{t}
over the set of all the unit directions
\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
. Therefore it is natural to ask: is there a similar equivalence relation if we replace the integral over
\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
by a finite sum of unit vectors? It seems this question can be answered when
\theta
takes value in the set of elementary unit vectors
\{e_{j}\}_{j=1}^{n}
, where each
e_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
has its
j
-th coordinate equal to
1
and all the other
(n-1)
coordinates equal to
0
. We use the notation
\varDelta^{L}_{t,j}f=\varDelta^{L}_{te_{j}}f
for
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}
, then for example, when
f
is a function defined on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
\varDelta^{1}_{t,1}f(x)=f(x_{1}+t,x_{2},\cdots,x_{n})-f(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{n})
. Denote

\tilde{\sigma}^{1}_{pq}=\max\{0,\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q}\},\qquad\sigma_{p}=\max\{0,n(\frac{1}{p}-1)\}.

(56)

Theorem 1.9
.

Let
L\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<p<\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
f\in\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

(i)If
0<p,q<\infty
,
\tilde{\sigma}^{1}_{pq}<s<L
, then for each
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}

\|(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-sq}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,j}f|^{q}\frac{dt}{t})^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(57)

(ii)Suppose
f
has a function representative. If
1<\min\{p,q\}
,
q<\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, or if
\min\{p,q\}\leq 1
,
q<\infty
and
\sigma_{pq}+\tilde{\sigma}^{1}_{pq}<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-sq}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,j}f(\cdot)|^{q}\frac{dt}{t})^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(58)

(iii)If
0<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
\frac{1}{p}<s<L
, then for each
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}

\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}\frac{|\varDelta^{L}_{t,j}f|}{t^{s}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(59)

(iv)Suppose
f
has a function representative. If
1<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, or if
0<p\leq 1
,
q=\infty
and
\sigma_{p}+\frac{1}{p}<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}\frac{|\varDelta^{L}_{t,j}f(\cdot)|}{t^{s}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(60)

Theorem
1.9
is new and it is worth noting that the condition in Theorem
1.9
(i) and the condition in Theorem
1.9
(ii) when
1<\min\{p,q\}
are completely independent of the dimension of the ambient space
\mathbb{R}^{n}
while the restriction of
s
in Theorem
1.9
(ii) when
\min\{p,q\}\leq 1
is only partially dependent on the dimension
n
. H. Triebel formulated the counterpart of Theorem
1.9
for the inhomogeneous
F^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
space in
[
8
, section 2.6.2]
with rough conditions
0<p<\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
and
\frac{n}{\min\{p,q\}}<s<M
. The proof of Theorem
1.9
can be found in section
6
. The corollaries of Theorem
1.9
are given below.

Corollary 1.10
.

Let
0<p<\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
f\in\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
has a function representative.

(i)If
0<p,q<\infty
,
\tilde{\sigma}^{1}_{pq}<s<1
, then for each
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{0}^{\infty}|f(x+te_{j})-f(x)|^{q}\frac{dt}{t^{1+sq}})^{\frac{p}{q}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(61)

(ii)If
1<\min\{p,q\}
,
q<\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, or if
\min\{p,q\}\leq 1
,
q<\infty
and
\sigma_{pq}+\tilde{\sigma}^{1}_{pq}<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{0}^{\infty}|f(x+te_{j})-f(x)|^{q}\frac{dt}{t^{1+sq}})^{\frac{p}{q}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

(62)

(iii)If
0<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
\frac{1}{p}<s<1
, then for each
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}\frac{|f(x+te_{j})-f(x)|^{p}}{t^{sp}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(63)

(iv)If
1<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, or if
0<p\leq 1
,
q=\infty
and
\sigma_{p}+\frac{1}{p}<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}\frac{|f(x+te_{j})-f(x)|^{p}}{t^{sp}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

(64)

Proof of Corollary
1.10
.

Apply inequalities (
57
), (
58
), (
59
) and (
60
) with
L=1
.
∎

Corollary 1.11
.

Let
0<p<\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
f\in\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
has a function representative.

(i)If
0<p,q<\infty
,
\tilde{\sigma}^{1}_{pq}<s<2
, then for each
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{0}^{\infty}|f(x+2te_{j})-2f(x+te_{j})+f(x)|^{q}\frac{dt}{t^{1+sq}})^{\frac{p}{q}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(65)

(ii)If
1<\min\{p,q\}
,
q<\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, or if
\min\{p,q\}\leq 1
,
q<\infty
and
\sigma_{pq}+\tilde{\sigma}^{1}_{pq}<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{0}^{\infty}|f(x+2te_{j})-2f(x+te_{j})+f(x)|^{q}\frac{dt}{t^{1+sq}})^{\frac{p}{q}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

(66)

(iii)If
0<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
\frac{1}{p}<s<2
, then for each
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}\frac{|f(x+2te_{j})-2f(x+te_{j})+f(x)|^{p}}{t^{sp}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(67)

(iv)If
1<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, or if
0<p\leq 1
,
q=\infty
and
\sigma_{p}+\frac{1}{p}<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}\frac{|f(x+2te_{j})-2f(x+te_{j})+f(x)|^{p}}{t^{sp}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

(68)

Proof of Corollary
1.11
.

Apply inequalities (
57
), (
58
), (
59
) and (
60
) with
L=2
.
∎

Finally, as part of a systematic study, we also state and prove the counterparts of Theorem
1.9
and Theorem
1.6
and their corollaries for
\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
spaces.

Theorem 1.12
.

Let
L\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<p,q\leq\infty
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
f\in\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

(i)If
0<p\leq\infty
,
0<q<\infty
and
0<s<L
, then for each
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}

(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-sq}\|\varDelta^{L}_{t,j}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dt}{t})^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(69)

(ii)Suppose
f
has a function representative. If
1<p\leq\infty
,
1\leq q<\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, or if
1<p\leq\infty
,
0<q<1
and
0<s<L
, or if
0<p\leq 1
,
0<q<\infty
and
\sigma_{p}<s<L
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-sq}\|\varDelta^{L}_{t,j}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dt}{t})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(70)

(iii)If
0<p\leq\infty
,
q=\infty
and
0<s<L
, then for each
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}

\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}t^{-s}\|\varDelta^{L}_{t,j}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(71)

(iv)Suppose
f
has a function representative. If
1<p\leq\infty
,
q=\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, or if
0<p\leq 1
,
q=\infty
and
\sigma_{p}<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}t^{-s}\|\varDelta^{L}_{t,j}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(72)

Theorem
1.12
is new. The proof of Theorem
1.12
is given in section
7
. The counterpart of Theorem
1.12
for the inhomogeneous
B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
space was obtained by H. Triebel in
[
8
, section 2.6.1]
with rough conditions
0<p,q\leq\infty
and
\sigma_{p}<s<M
. The corollaries of Theorem
1.12
are formulated below.

Corollary 1.13
.

Let
0<p,q<\infty
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
f\in\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
has a function representative.

(i)If
0<p<\infty
,
0<q<\infty
and
0<s<1
, then for each
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}

(\int_{0}^{\infty}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+te_{j})-f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{q}{p}}\frac{dt}{t^{1+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(73)

(ii)If
1<p<\infty
,
1\leq q<\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, or if
1<p<\infty
,
0<q<1
and
0<s<1
, or if
0<p\leq 1
,
0<q<\infty
and
\sigma_{p}<s<1
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\int_{0}^{\infty}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+te_{j})-f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{q}{p}}\frac{dt}{t^{1+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(74)

Proof of Corollary
1.13
.

Apply Theorem
1.12
(i) and (ii) with
L=1
. And (
74
) also indicates the following inequality

2^{ks}\|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\int_{0}^{\infty}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+te_{j})-f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{q}{p}}\frac{dt}{t^{1+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}

(75)

for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, and hence
\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}\|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=0
when
s>0
and the right side of (
74
) is finite.
∎

Corollary 1.14
.

Let
0<p,q\leq\infty
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
and at least one of
p
and
q
is infinity. Assume
f\in\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
has a function representative.

(i)If
p=\infty
,
0<q<\infty
and
0<s<1
, then for each
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}

(\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+te_{j})-f(x)|^{q}\frac{dt}{t^{1+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(76)

(ii)If
p=\infty
,
1\leq q<\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, or if
p=\infty
,
0<q<1
and
0<s<1
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+te_{j})-f(x)|^{q}\frac{dt}{t^{1+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(77)

(iii)If
0<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
0<s<1
, then for each
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}

\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}t^{-s}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+te_{j})-f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(78)

(iv)If
1<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, or if
0<p\leq 1
,
q=\infty
and
\sigma_{p}<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}t^{-s}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+te_{j})-f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

(79)

(v)If
p=q=\infty
and
0<s<1
, then for each
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}

\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x+te_{j})-f(x)|}{t^{s}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(80)

(vi)If
p=q=\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x+te_{j})-f(x)|}{t^{s}}.

(81)

Proof of Corollary
1.14
.

Apply Theorem
1.12
with
L=1
. We also deduce from (
76
) that for almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

(\int_{0}^{\infty}|f(x+te_{j})-f(x)|^{q}\frac{dt}{t^{1+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

(82)

when conditions of Corollary
1.14
(i) are satisfied. Furthermore if
0<p\leq\infty
,
q=\infty
and
0<s<1
, then we can infer from (
78
) and (
80
) the following inequality

\|f(\cdot+te_{j})-f(\cdot)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim t^{s}\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\qquad\text{ for }t>0.

(83)

Moreover when conditions of Corollary
1.14
(iv) are satisfied, the inequality (
79
) indicates

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}\frac{|f(x+te_{j})-f(x)|^{p}}{t^{sp}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

(84)

From (
77
) and (
81
) we see that for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
2^{ks}|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f(x)|
can be estimated from above by the right sides of (
77
) and (
81
) respectively, and hence we deduce
\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}\psi_{2^{-k}}*f(x)=0
when
s>0
and the right sides of (
77
) and (
81
) are finite. From (
79
) we see that for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}

\displaystyle 2^{ks}\|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}t^{-s}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+te_{j})-f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}\frac{|f(x+te_{j})-f(x)|^{p}}{t^{sp}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}},

(85)

and hence we deduce
\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}\|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=0
when
s>0
and the right side of (
1
) is finite.
∎

Corollary 1.15
.

Let
0<p,q<\infty
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
f\in\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
has a function representative.

(i)If
0<p<\infty
,
0<q<\infty
and
0<s<2
, then for each
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}

(\int_{0}^{\infty}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+2te_{j})-2f(x+te_{j})+f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{q}{p}}\frac{dt}{t^{1+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(86)

(ii)If
1<p<\infty
,
1\leq q<\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, or if
1<p<\infty
,
0<q<1
and
0<s<2
, or if
0<p\leq 1
,
0<q<\infty
and
\sigma_{p}<s<2
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\int_{0}^{\infty}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+2te_{j})-2f(x+te_{j})+f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{q}{p}}\frac{dt}{t^{1+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(87)

Proof of Corollary
1.15
.

Apply Theorem
1.12
(i) and (ii) with
L=2
. And (
87
) also indicates the following inequality

\displaystyle 2^{ks}\|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\int_{0}^{\infty}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+2te_{j})-2f(x+te_{j})+f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{q}{p}}\frac{dt}{t^{1+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}

(88)

for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, and hence
\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}\|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=0
when
s>0
and the right side of (
87
) is finite.
∎

Corollary 1.16
.

Let
0<p,q\leq\infty
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
and at least one of
p
and
q
is infinity. Assume
f\in\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
has a function representative.

(i)If
p=\infty
,
0<q<\infty
and
0<s<2
, then for each
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}

(\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+2te_{j})-2f(x+te_{j})+f(x)|^{q}\frac{dt}{t^{1+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(89)

(ii)If
p=\infty
,
1\leq q<\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, or if
p=\infty
,
0<q<1
and
0<s<2
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+2te_{j})-2f(x+te_{j})+f(x)|^{q}\frac{dt}{t^{1+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(90)

(iii)If
0<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
0<s<2
, then for each
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}

\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}t^{-s}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+2te_{j})-2f(x+te_{j})+f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(91)

(iv)If
1<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, or if
0<p\leq 1
,
q=\infty
and
\sigma_{p}<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}t^{-s}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+2te_{j})-2f(x+te_{j})+f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

(92)

(v)If
p=q=\infty
and
0<s<2
, then for each
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}

\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}t^{-s}|f(x+2te_{j})-2f(x+te_{j})+f(x)|\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(93)

(vi)If
p=q=\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}t^{-s}|f(x+2te_{j})-2f(x+te_{j})+f(x)|.

(94)

Proof of Corollary
1.16
.

Apply Theorem
1.12
with
L=2
. We also deduce from (
89
) that for almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,

(\int_{0}^{\infty}|f(x+2te_{j})-2f(x+te_{j})+f(x)|^{q}\frac{dt}{t^{1+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

(95)

when conditions of Corollary
1.16
(i) are satisfied. Furthermore if
0<p\leq\infty
,
q=\infty
and
0<s<2
, then we can infer from (
91
) and (
93
) the following inequality

\|f(\cdot+2te_{j})-2f(\cdot+te_{j})+f(\cdot)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim t^{s}\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\qquad\text{ for }t>0.

(96)

Moreover when conditions of Corollary
1.16
(iv) are satisfied, the inequality (
92
) indicates

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}\frac{|f(x+2te_{j})-2f(x+te_{j})+f(x)|^{p}}{t^{sp}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

(97)

From (
90
) and (
94
) we see that for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
2^{ks}|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f(x)|
can be estimated from above by the right sides of (
90
) and (
94
) respectively, and hence we deduce
\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}\psi_{2^{-k}}*f(x)=0
when
s>0
and the right sides of (
90
) and (
94
) are finite. From (
92
) we see that for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}

\displaystyle 2^{ks}\|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}t^{-s}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+2te_{j})-2f(x+te_{j})+f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}\frac{|f(x+2te_{j})-2f(x+te_{j})+f(x)|^{p}}{t^{sp}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}},

(98)

and hence we deduce
\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}\|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=0
when
s>0
and the right side of (
1
) is finite.
∎

The following theorem is the counterpart of Theorem
1.6
for
\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
spaces.

Theorem 1.17
.

Let
L\in\mathbb{N}
,
0<p,q\leq\infty
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
f\in\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
.

(i)If
0<p\leq\infty
,
0<q<\infty
and
0<s<L
, then

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-sq}\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(99)

(ii)Suppose
f
has a function representative. If
1<p\leq\infty
,
0<q<\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, or if
0<p<1
,
0<q<\infty
and
\sigma_{p}<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-sq}\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n}})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(100)

(iii)If
0<p\leq\infty
,
q=\infty
and
0<s<L
, then

\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-s}\cdot\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(101)

(iv)Suppose
f
has a function representative. If
1<p\leq\infty
,
q=\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, or if
0<p<1
,
q=\infty
and
\sigma_{p}<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-s}\cdot\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(102)

(v)Suppose
f
has a function representative. If
p=1
,
1\leq q<\infty
and
-n<s<\infty
, or if
p=1
,
0<q<1
and
0<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{1,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-sq}\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n}})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(103)

If
p=1
,
q=\infty
and
-n<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{1,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-s}\cdot\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(104)

Theorem
1.17
(v) and the case for
1<p\leq\infty
in Theorem
1.17
(ii) and (iv) are new. The proof of Theorem
1.17
can be found in section
8
. Initial considerations of the counterpart of Theorem
1.17
for the inhomogeneous
B^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
space were given by H. Triebel in
[
7
, section 2.5.12]
and
[
8
, section 2.6.1]
. The corollaries of Theorem
1.17
are formulated below.

Corollary 1.18
.

Let
0<p,q<\infty
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
f\in\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
has a function representative.

(i)If
0<p<\infty
,
0<q<\infty
and
0<s<1
, then

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+h)-f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{q}{p}}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(105)

(ii)If
1<p<\infty
,
0<q<\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, or if
0<p<1
,
0<q<\infty
and
\sigma_{p}<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+h)-f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{q}{p}}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(106)

(iii)If
p=1
,
1\leq q<\infty
and
-n<s<\infty
, or if
p=1
,
0<q<1
and
0<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{1,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+h)-f(x)|dx)^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(107)

Proof of Corollary
1.18
.

Apply inequalities (
99
), (
100
) and (
103
) with
L=1
. Inequalities (
106
) and (
107
) also indicate

2^{ks}\|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+h)-f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{q}{p}}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}},

(108)

for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, and hence
\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}\|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=0
when
s>0
and the right sides of (
106
) and (
107
) are finite.
∎

We can also pick some special values for
p
,
q
,
s
in the above inequalities and then deduce some other interesting inequalities. For example, let
1\leq q\leq p<\frac{n}{s}
, then
0<s<1
and
s<\frac{n}{p}
. By Lemma
2.11
, we have
\|f_{k}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim 2^{kn(\frac{1}{q}-\frac{1}{p})}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
and hence

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\big(\sum_{k\leq 0}2^{kq(s+\frac{n}{q}-\frac{n}{p})}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}+\sum_{k>0}2^{kq(s+\frac{n}{q}-\frac{n}{p})}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\big)^{\frac{1}{q}}

(109)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\big(\sum_{k\leq 0}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\big)^{\frac{1}{q}}+\big(\sum_{k>0}2^{kn}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\big)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{0}_{q,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{\frac{n}{q}}_{q,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

By the inequalities given in Corollary
1.18
and Fubini’s theorem, we can further deduce the following inequality

\displaystyle(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+h)-f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{q}{p}}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

(110)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{q}}{|x-y|^{n}}dxdy\big)^{\frac{1}{q}}+\big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{q}}{|x-y|^{2n}}dxdy\big)^{\frac{1}{q}},

when the corresponding conditions on the parameters are satisfied.

Corollary 1.19
.

Let
0<p,q\leq\infty
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
and at least one of
p
and
q
is infinity. Assume
f\in\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
has a function representative.

(i)If
p=\infty
,
0<q<\infty
and
0<s<1
, then

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x+h)-f(x)|^{q}}{|h|^{n+sq}}dh)^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(111)

(ii)If
p=\infty
,
0<q<\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x+h)-f(x)|^{q}}{|h|^{n+sq}}dh)^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(112)

(iii)If
0<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
0<s<1
, then

\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-s}\cdot(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+h)-f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(113)

(iv)If
1<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, or if
p=1
,
q=\infty
and
-n<s<\infty
, or if
0<p<1
,
q=\infty
and
\sigma_{p}<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-s}\cdot(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+h)-f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

(114)

(v)If
p=q=\infty
and
0<s<1
, then

\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|}{|x-y|^{s}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(115)

(vi)If
p=q=\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|}{|x-y|^{s}}.

(116)

Proof of Corollary
1.19
.

Apply Theorem
1.17
with
L=1
. From (
111
) we can see that

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x+h)-f(x)|^{q}}{|h|^{n+sq}}dh)^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

(117)

for almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
when conditions of Corollary
1.19
(i) are satisfied. From (
113
) and (
115
), we also deduce the following inequality

\|f(\cdot+h)-f(\cdot)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim|h|^{s}\cdot\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

(118)

for almost every
h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
when
0<p\leq\infty
,
q=\infty
and
0<s<1
. From (
114
) with a proper change of variable, we can obtain

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{p}}{|x-y|^{sp}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}},

(119)

when conditions of Corollary
1.19
(iv) are satisfied. Furthermore from (
112
) we have

2^{ks}|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f(x)|\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x+h)-f(x)|^{q}}{|h|^{n+sq}}dh)^{\frac{1}{q}}

(120)

for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and from (
116
) we have

2^{ks}|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f(x)|\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|}{|x-y|^{s}}

(121)

for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, therefore
\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f(x)|=0
when
s>0
and the right sides of (
112
) and (
116
) are finite. Moreover from (
114
) we have

2^{ks}\|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-s}\cdot(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+h)-f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}

(122)

for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, hence
\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}\|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=0
when
s>0
and the right side of (
114
) is finite.
∎

If
0<\alpha\leq p<\infty
,
0<s<1
and
0<\beta<\infty
, then by Lemma
2.11
,
f_{k}=\psi_{2^{-k}}*f
satisfies
\|f_{k}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim 2^{kn(\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{p})}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
, and we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(s+\frac{n}{\alpha}-\frac{n}{p})}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(s+\frac{n}{\alpha}-\frac{n}{p})}|f_{k}(x)|)^{\alpha}dx\big)^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k\beta(s+\frac{n}{\alpha}-\frac{n}{p})}|f_{k}(x)|^{\beta})^{\frac{\alpha}{\beta}}dx\big)^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}=\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s+\frac{n}{\alpha}-\frac{n}{p}}_{\alpha,\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(123)

By combining (
113
), (
123
), and (
47
) altogether, we can obtain

\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\|f(\cdot+h)-f(\cdot)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}}{|h|^{s}}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{\beta}}{|x-y|^{n+\beta(s+\frac{n}{\alpha}-\frac{n}{p})}}dy)^{\frac{\alpha}{\beta}}dx)^{\frac{1}{\alpha}},

(124)

where the parameters satisfy

\max\{0,n(\frac{1}{\min\{\alpha,\beta\}}-1)\}+\max\{0,n(\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{\beta})\}<s+\frac{n}{\alpha}-\frac{n}{p}

if
0<\beta<1
, and there are no extra conditions for parameters if
1\leq\beta<\infty
since
-n<s+\frac{n}{\alpha}-\frac{n}{p}<\infty
is always true for
0<\alpha\leq p<\infty
and
0<s<1
. In particular, letting
\alpha=p
in (
124
) yields

\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\|f(\cdot+h)-f(\cdot)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}}{|h|^{s}}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{\beta}}{|x-y|^{n+s\beta}}dy)^{\frac{p}{\beta}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}=[f]_{W^{s}_{p,\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

(125)

when the above conditions are met. If
0<\alpha<p=\infty
,
0<s<1
, and
0<\beta<\infty
, then by Lemma
2.11
we have
\|f_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim 2^{\frac{kn}{\alpha}}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
and

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(s+\frac{n}{\alpha})}\|f_{k}\|_{L^{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s+\frac{n}{\alpha}}_{\alpha,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(126)

By (
115
), (
126
), and (
49
), we have

\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|}{|x-y|^{s}}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{\alpha}}{|x-y|^{n+s\alpha}}dx)^{\frac{1}{\alpha}}

(127)

for all
0<\alpha<\infty
and
0<s<1
. Because
\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s+\frac{n}{\alpha}}_{\alpha,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s+\frac{n}{\alpha}}_{\alpha,\beta}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
for all
0<\beta<\infty
, then (
115
), (
126
) and (
47
) combined together give us the following inequality

\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|}{|x-y|^{s}}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{\beta}}{|x-y|^{n+(s+\frac{n}{\alpha})\beta}}dy)^{\frac{\alpha}{\beta}}dx)^{\frac{1}{\alpha}},

(128)

where the parameters satisfy

\max\{0,n(\frac{1}{\min\{\alpha,\beta\}}-1)\}+\max\{0,n(\frac{1}{\alpha}-\frac{1}{\beta})\}<s+\frac{n}{\alpha}

if
0<\beta<1
, and there are no extra conditions for parameters if
1\leq\beta<\infty
. In particular, when
\alpha
and
\beta
are related by the equation
\beta=\alpha\cdot\gamma
for some
\gamma>0
, then (
128
) becomes

\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|}{|x-y|^{s}}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)-f(y)|^{\alpha\cdot\gamma}}{|x-y|^{n+n\gamma+s\alpha\cdot\gamma}}dy)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}dx)^{\frac{\gamma}{\beta}},

(129)

when the corresponding conditions are satisfied.

Corollary 1.20
.

Let
0<p,q<\infty
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
and
f\in\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
has a function representative.

(i)If
0<p<\infty
,
0<q<\infty
and
0<s<2
, then

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+2h)-2f(x+h)+f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{q}{p}}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(130)

(ii)If
1<p<\infty
,
0<q<\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, or if
0<p<1
,
0<q<\infty
and
\sigma_{p}<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+2h)-2f(x+h)+f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{q}{p}}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(131)

(iii)If
p=1
,
1\leq q<\infty
and
-n<s<\infty
, or if
p=1
,
0<q<1
and
0<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{1,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+2h)-2f(x+h)+f(x)|dx)^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(132)

Proof of Corollary
1.20
.

Apply inequalities (
99
), (
100
) and (
103
) with
L=2
. Inequalities (
131
) and (
132
) also indicate

2^{ks}\|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+2h)-2f(x+h)+f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{q}{p}}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}},

(133)

for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, and hence
\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}\|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=0
when
s>0
and the right sides of (
131
) and (
132
) are finite.
∎

Corollary 1.21
.

Let
0<p,q\leq\infty
,
s\in\mathbb{R}
and at least one of
p
and
q
is infinity. Assume
f\in\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
has a function representative.

(i)If
p=\infty
,
0<q<\infty
and
0<s<2
, then

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+2h)-2f(x+h)+f(x)|^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(134)

(ii)If
p=\infty
,
0<q<\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+2h)-2f(x+h)+f(x)|^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(135)

(iii)If
0<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
0<s<2
, then

\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-s}\cdot(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+2h)-2f(x+h)+f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(136)

(iv)If
1<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, or if
p=1
,
q=\infty
and
-n<s<\infty
, or if
0<p<1
,
q=\infty
and
\sigma_{p}<s<\infty
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-s}\cdot(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+2h)-2f(x+h)+f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

(137)

(v)If
p=q=\infty
and
0<s<2
, then

\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)+f(y)-2f(\frac{x+y}{2})|}{|x-y|^{s}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(138)

(vi)If
p=q=\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, then

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)+f(y)-2f(\frac{x+y}{2})|}{|x-y|^{s}}.

(139)

Proof of Corollary
1.21
.

Apply Theorem
1.17
with
L=2
. From (
134
) we can see that

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+2h)-2f(x+h)+f(x)|^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{\infty,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

(140)

for almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
when conditions of Corollary
1.21
(i) are satisfied. From (
136
) and (
138
), we also deduce the following inequality

\|f(\cdot+2h)-2f(\cdot+h)+f(\cdot)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim|h|^{s}\cdot\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

(141)

for almost every
h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
when
0<p\leq\infty
,
q=\infty
and
0<s<2
. From (
137
) with a proper change of variable, we can obtain

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)+f(y)-2f(\frac{x+y}{2})|^{p}}{|x-y|^{sp}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}},

(142)

when conditions of Corollary
1.21
(iv) are satisfied. Furthermore from (
135
) we have

2^{ks}|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f(x)|\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+2h)-2f(x+h)+f(x)|^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n+sq}})^{\frac{1}{q}}

(143)

for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and from (
139
) we have

2^{ks}|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f(x)|\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x)+f(y)-2f(\frac{x+y}{2})|}{|x-y|^{s}}

(144)

for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, therefore
\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f(x)|=0
when
s>0
and the right sides of (
135
) and (
139
) are finite. Moreover from (
137
) we have

2^{ks}\|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-s}\cdot(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|f(x+2h)-2f(x+h)+f(x)|^{p}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}

(145)

for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, hence
\lim_{k\rightarrow+\infty}\|\psi_{2^{-k}}*f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=0
when
s>0
and the right side of (
137
) is finite.
∎

2.
Preliminaries

In this section, we continue introducing some more notations and definitions. In order to write this paper in a more self-included fashion, we also cite useful results directly from the literature and the proofs of cited results can be found from their respective source and then we deduce frequently used remarks right after the closely related citations. Let
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
be the set of smooth functions on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
with compact supports and if a function
f
is in
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, we use
spt.f
to denote the support set of this function. The notation “
X\lesssim Y
” means
X
is dominated by a constant multiple of
Y
and the constant is determined by some fixed parameters, and when we want to emphasize the constant is
1
we still use the usual notation “
X\leq Y
”. If
X\lesssim Y
and
Y\lesssim X
, then we consider
X
and
Y
are equivalent and write
X\sim Y
. For a sufficiently smooth function
f
and a multi-index
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{n})
, we denote the derivative by

\partial^{\alpha}f(x)=\frac{\partial^{|\alpha|}f(x)}{\partial x_{1}^{\alpha_{1}}\partial x_{2}^{\alpha_{2}}\cdots\partial x_{n}^{\alpha_{n}}}.

We also recall the definition of the famous Hardy-Littlewood maximal function.

Definition 2.1
.

If a function
f
is locally integrable on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, then

\mathcal{M}_{n}(f)(x):=\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{\delta>0}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0ptB(x,\delta)}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{B(x,\delta)}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{B(x,\delta)}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{B(x,\delta)}}|f(y)|dy

is the
n
-dimensional Hardy-Littlewood maximal function of
f
at
x
, and
B(x,\delta)
is the
n
-dimensional ball centered at
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and has radius
\delta
.

We define the Peetre-Fefferman-Stein maximal function for a function, the Fourier transform of which has compact supports in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Definition 2.2
.

If
f
is a function defined on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
whose distributional Fourier transform is compactly supported in the ball
B(0,t)\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
centered at origin with radius
t>0
, then the associated
n
-dimensional Peetre-Fefferman-Stein maximal function of
f
at
x
is given by

\mathcal{P}_{n}f(x)=\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x-z)|}{(1+t|z|)^{\frac{n}{r}}}

where in most cases of this paper we pick
r
to be a positive number satisfying either
0<r<\min\{p,q\}
or
0<r<p
.

In general, the
n
-dimensional Peetre-Fefferman-Stein maximal function can be defined as

\mathcal{P}_{n}f(x)=\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x-z)|}{(1+t|z|)^{a}}

for any positive real number
a
but for the convenience of notations in this paper, we choose
a=\frac{n}{r}
for the specified
r
.

Remark 2.3
.

For the Fourier transform, Hardy-Littlewood maximal function and Peetre-Fefferman-Stein maximal function, when we want to apply these operations only to some specific coordinates, we use a subscript number different from the dimension of the ambient space
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For example if
f(x)\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for
n>1
, let
x=(x_{1},x_{2},\cdots,x_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and we denote
x_{1}^{\prime}=(x_{2},\cdots,x_{n})
then
x=(x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime})
and
f(x)=f(x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime})
. If the
1
-dimensional Fourier transform is done with respect to
x_{1}
then we use the notation

\mathcal{F}_{1}f(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})(y_{1}):=\int_{\mathbb{R}}f(x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime})e^{-2\pi ix_{1}y_{1}}dx_{1},

and if the
(n-1)
-dimensional Fourier transform is done with respect to
x_{1}^{\prime}
then we use the notation

\mathcal{F}_{n-1}f(x_{1},\cdot)(y_{1}^{\prime}):=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}f(x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime})e^{-2\pi ix_{1}^{\prime}\cdot y_{1}^{\prime}}dx_{1}^{\prime},

where
y_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
and
x_{1}^{\prime}\cdot y_{1}^{\prime}
is the inner product in
\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
,
dx_{1}^{\prime}=dx_{2}\cdots dx_{n}
. Similar notations are used for the inverse Fourier transforms. If we fix
x_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
then the
1
-dimensional Hardy-Littlewood maximal function of
f
, with respect to the first coordinate, centered at
u\in\mathbb{R}
is given by

\mathcal{M}_{1}(f(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime}))(u):=\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{\delta>0}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0pt-\delta<t<\delta}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{-\delta<t<\delta}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{-\delta<t<\delta}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{-\delta<t<\delta}}|f(u+t,x_{1}^{\prime})|dt,

and if furthermore the
1
-dimensional Fourier transform
\mathcal{F}_{1}f(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})(u)
is supported in the interval
\{u\in\mathbb{R}:|u|<t\}
,
t\in\mathbb{R}
, then we can also define the associated
1
-dimensional Peetre-Fefferman-Stein maximal function of
f
at
u
, with respect to the first coordinate, as follows

\mathcal{P}_{1}f(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})(u)=\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{|f(u-z,x_{1}^{\prime})|}{(1+t|z|)^{\frac{1}{r}}}.

Remark 2.4
.

Directly from Definition
2.2
and the use of some basic inequalities we can obtain useful inequalities for the Peetre-Fefferman-Stein maximal function. If
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
then

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{n}f(x-y)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x-y-z)|}{(1+t|z+y|)^{n/r}}\cdot\frac{(1+t|y+z|)^{n/r}}{(1+t|z|)^{n/r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(x-y-z)|}{(1+t|z+y|)^{n/r}}\cdot\frac{(1+t|y|)^{n/r}\cdot(1+t|z|)^{n/r}}{(1+t|z|)^{n/r}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{n}f(x)\cdot(1+t|y|)^{n/r}.

And also, using the above inequality gives that

\mathcal{P}_{n}f(x)=\mathcal{P}_{n}f(x-y+y)\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{n}f(x-y)\cdot(1+t|y|)^{n/r}.

Therefore we reach the conclusion that for
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

\mathcal{P}_{n}f(x)\cdot(1+t|y|)^{-n/r}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{n}f(x-y)\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{n}f(x)\cdot(1+t|y|)^{n/r},

(146)

where the constants involved are independent of
t
and
y
. We also infer from (
146
) that if
\mathcal{P}_{n}f(x)
vanishes at some point
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
then the Peetre-Fefferman-Stein maximal function
\mathcal{P}_{n}f
vanishes on all of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and hence
f
vanishes on all of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. So for a nonzero function
f
, the associated Peetre-Fefferman-Stein maximal function
\mathcal{P}_{n}f
is positive everywhere. And it would be obvious that if
\mathcal{P}_{n}f(x)=\infty
for some
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
then
\mathcal{P}_{n}f=\infty
on all of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
.

Remark 2.5
.

If
f
is a function defined on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and its distributional Fourier transform satisfies
spt.\mathcal{F}_{n}f\subseteq B(0,t)
for
t>0
and
\varphi
is a Schwartz function whose Fourier transform is compactly supported in
B(0,1)\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\varphi_{1/t}(x)=t^{n}\varphi(tx)
then the distributional Fourier transform of the convolution
\varphi_{1/t}*f
is also compactly supported in
B(0,t)\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{n}(\varphi_{1/t}*f)(x)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|\varphi_{1/t}*f(x-z)|}{(1+t|z|)^{n/r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|\varphi_{1/t}(y)f(x-z-y)|\cdot(1+t|z+y|)^{n/r}}{(1+t|z|)^{n/r}(1+t|z+y|)^{n/r}}dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|\varphi_{1/t}(y)f(x-z-y)|\cdot(1+t|y|)^{n/r}}{(1+t|z+y|)^{n/r}}dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{n}f(x)\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\varphi_{1/t}(y)|\cdot(1+t|y|)^{n/r}dy,

that is,

\mathcal{P}_{n}(\varphi_{1/t}*f)(x)\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{n}f(x)

(147)

for
t>0,x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and the constant is independent of
t
.

For the reader’s convenience we also would like to cite some useful results from the well-known literature
[
2
,
3
]
below.

Lemma 2.6

(cf. Lemma 2.2.3 of
[
3
]
)
.

Let
0<r<\infty
. Then there exist constants
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
such that for all
t>0
and for all
\mathscr{C}^{1}
functions
u
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
whose distributional Fourier transform is supported in the ball
|\xi|\leq t
we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{1}{t}\frac{|\nabla u(x-z)|}{(1+t|z|)^{\frac{n}{r}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|u(x-z)|}{(1+t|z|)^{\frac{n}{r}}},

(148)

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|u(x-z)|}{(1+t|z|)^{\frac{n}{r}}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{2}\mathcal{M}_{n}(|u|^{r})(x)^{\frac{1}{r}},

(149)

where
\mathcal{M}_{n}
denotes the Hardy-Littlewood maximal operator. The constants
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
depend only on the dimension
n
and
r
; in particular, they are independent of
t
.

Remark 2.7
.

The above Lemma
2.6
is significant in the sense that it provides a pointwise estimate by the famous Hardy-Littlewood maximal function to a function
u
whose distributional Fourier transform has compact support in the ball
B(0,t)
of center
0
and radius
t
and we have the following

|u(x)|\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|u(x-z)|}{(1+t|z|)^{\frac{n}{r}}}=\mathcal{P}_{n}u(x)\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{n}(|u|^{r})(x)^{\frac{1}{r}}

where
r
can be a positive finite number chosen to satisfy particular needs.

Remark 2.8
.

Assuming sufficient smoothness of the function
u
as a tempered distribution in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and iterating (
148
) of Lemma
2.6
repeatedly, since for any multi-index
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{n})
, the distributional Fourier transform of
\partial^{\alpha}u
is also supported in
B(0,t)\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we obtain

\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{1}{t^{|\alpha|}}\frac{|\partial^{\alpha}u(x-z)|}{(1+t|z|)^{\frac{n}{r}}}\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|u(x-z)|}{(1+t|z|)^{\frac{n}{r}}}

(150)

and the constant is independent of
t
.

Lemma 2.9

(cf. Corollary 2.2.4 of
[
3
]
)
.

Let
0<p\leq\infty
and
\alpha
a multi-index. Then there are constants
C=C(\alpha,n,p)
and
C^{\prime}=C(\alpha,n,p)
such that for all Schwartz functions
u
on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
whose Fourier transform is supported in the ball
B(0,t)
, for some
t>0
, we have

\|\partial^{\alpha}u\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq Ct^{|\alpha|}\|u\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

(151)

and

\|\partial^{\alpha}u\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq C^{\prime}t^{|\alpha|+\frac{n}{p}}\|u\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(152)

Remark 2.10
.

Let
p,u,t,\alpha
be given as in Lemma
2.9
and
q\in\mathbb{R}
satisfies
0<p\leq q\leq\infty
then a simple interpolation with (
151
) and (
152
) reveals that

\|\partial^{\alpha}u\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim t^{|\alpha|+n(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q})}\|u\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

(153)

where
u
is a Schwartz function on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\mathcal{F}_{n}u
is supported in the ball
B(0,t)
. This inequality is also known as the Plancherel-Polya-Nikol’skij inequality and the constant on the right side of (
153
) only depends on
\alpha,n,p,q
. For a more general introduction to the Plancherel-Polya-Nikol’skij inequality, we would like to refer the interested reader to section 1.3 of
[
7
]
.

The Plancherel-Polya-Nikol’skij inequality can be generalized to the class of sufficiently smooth functions that are also tempered distributions.

Lemma 2.11
.

Suppose
u(x)
defined on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
is a sufficiently smooth function as a tempered distribution in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, and its
n
-dimensional distributional Fourier transform is supported in the ball
\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|\xi|\leq t\}
for some
t>0
. Assume
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\cdots,\alpha_{n})
is a multi-index and
0<p\leq q\leq\infty
, then

\|\partial^{\alpha}u\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim t^{|\alpha|+n(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q})}\|u\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

(154)

and the constant on the right side of (
154
) only depends on
\alpha,n,p,q
.

Proof.

Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfy the following conditions

0\leq\mathcal{F}_{n}\varphi\leq 1,\text{ }spt.\mathcal{F}_{n}\varphi\subseteq\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|\xi|\leq 2\}\text{ and }\mathcal{F}_{n}\varphi=1\text{ if }|\xi|\leq 1.

Then
\mathcal{F}_{n}\varphi_{\frac{1}{t}}(\xi)=\mathcal{F}_{n}\varphi(\frac{\xi}{t})
is supported in
\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|\xi|\leq 2t\}
and equals
1
if
|\xi|\leq t
. Let
g
be an arbitrary element in
\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, then

<u,g>=<\mathcal{F}_{n}u,\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}g>=<\mathcal{F}_{n}u,\mathcal{F}_{n}\varphi_{\frac{1}{t}}\cdot\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}g>+<\mathcal{F}_{n}u,(1-\mathcal{F}_{n}\varphi_{\frac{1}{t}})\cdot\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}g>.

The Schwartz function
(1-\mathcal{F}_{n}\varphi_{\frac{1}{t}})\cdot\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}g
is supported in
\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|\xi|>t\}
and the distributional Fourier transform
\mathcal{F}_{n}u
is supported in
\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|\xi|\leq t\}
, then

<\mathcal{F}_{n}u,(1-\mathcal{F}_{n}\varphi_{\frac{1}{t}})\cdot\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}g>=0.

Hence we obtain the following

<u,g>=<\mathcal{F}_{n}u,\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\tilde{\varphi}_{\frac{1}{t}}*g)>=<\varphi_{\frac{1}{t}}*u,g>,

(155)

where
\tilde{\varphi}(x)=\varphi(-x)
. Equation (
155
) shows that
u(x)=\varphi_{\frac{1}{t}}*u(x)
for almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. If
1\leq p\leq\infty
, using Hölder’s inequality, we have

\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\leq\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|u(y)|\cdot t^{n}|\varphi(tx-ty)|dy\leq t^{\frac{n}{p}}\|\varphi\|_{L^{p^{\prime}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\cdot\|u\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

(156)

where
p^{\prime}
is the Hölder’s conjugate of
p
. If
0<p<1
, then we have

\displaystyle\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq t^{n}\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|u(y)|^{p}\cdot|u(y)|^{1-p}dy

\displaystyle\leq t^{n}\|\varphi\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\cdot\|u\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{1-p}\cdot\|u\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{p}.

(157)

If
0<p\leq q\leq\infty
, we then use (
156
) and (
2
) to get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|u(x)|^{q}dx

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|u(x)|^{q-p}\cdot|u(x)|^{p}dx

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{\frac{n}{p}(q-p)}\|u\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q-p}\cdot\|u\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{p}=t^{\frac{n}{p}(q-p)}\|u\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}.

(158)

Thus we can obtain (
154
) if
|\alpha|=0
. When
u
is sufficiently smooth and
|\alpha|>0
, we use Remark
2.8
and Lemma
2.6
to obtain

|\partial^{\alpha}u(x)|\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{n}(\partial^{\alpha}u)(x)\lesssim t^{|\alpha|}\mathcal{P}_{n}(u)(x)\lesssim t^{|\alpha|}\mathcal{M}_{n}(|u|^{r})(x)^{\frac{1}{r}},

(159)

where the constants depend on
n
and
r
. By picking
r<q
and invoking the mapping property of the Hardy-Littlewood maximal function, we have

\|\partial^{\alpha}u\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim t^{|\alpha|}\|\mathcal{M}_{n}(|u|^{r})(x)^{\frac{1}{r}}\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim t^{|\alpha|}\|u\|_{L^{q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim t^{|\alpha|+n(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q})}\|u\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

(160)

where constants depend only on
n,r,p,q
and are independent of
t
.
∎

An example that shows the advantage of Lemma
2.11
over Remark
2.10
can be given below. Consider the function
f_{j}=f*\psi_{2^{-j}}
where
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
\psi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
satisfies (
1
), (
2
). Then for each
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, the distributional Fourier transform of
f_{j}
is supported in
\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:2^{j-1}\leq|\xi|<2^{j+1}\}
and by Theorem 2.3.20 of
[
2
]
,
f_{j}
is a smooth function which has at most polynomial growth at infinity. Then
f_{j}
is a smooth tempered distribution and applying Lemma
2.11
, we have

f_{j}(x)\lesssim\|f_{j}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim 2^{jn/p}\|f_{j}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\quad\text{for all }0<p<\infty.

Lemma 2.12

(cf. Theorem 5.6.6 of
[
2
]
)
.

For
1<p,r<\infty
the Hardy-Littlewood maximal function
\mathcal{M}
satisfies the vector-valued inequalities

\displaystyle\|(\sum_{j}|\mathcal{M}(f_{j})|^{r})^{\frac{1}{r}}\|_{L^{1,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}_{n}(1+(r-1)^{-1})\|(\sum_{j}|f_{j}|^{r})^{\frac{1}{r}}\|_{L^{1}},

(161)

\displaystyle\|(\sum_{j}|\mathcal{M}(f_{j})|^{r})^{\frac{1}{r}}\|_{L^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{n}c(p,r)\|(\sum_{j}|f_{j}|^{r})^{\frac{1}{r}}\|_{L^{p}},

(162)

where
c(p,r)=(1+(r-1)^{-1})(p+(p-1)^{-1})
.

Remark 2.13
.

Recall that for each
l\in\mathbb{Z}
the distributional Fourier transform of
f_{l}:=\psi_{2^{-l}}*f
is supported in the compact annulus
2^{l-1}\leq|\xi|<2^{l+1}
and

\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{l}(x)=\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|\psi_{2^{-l}}*f(x-z)|}{(1+2^{l+1}|z|)^{\frac{n}{r}}}.

Applying Lemma
2.6
first and then Lemma
2.12
and assuming that
s\in\mathbb{R},0<p,q<\infty,0<r<\min\{p,q\}
we obtain

\displaystyle(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\sum_{l=-\infty}^{\infty}|2^{ls}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{l}(y)|^{q})^{\frac{p}{q}}dy)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\sum_{l=-\infty}^{\infty}|2^{ls}\mathcal{M}_{n}(|f_{l}|^{r})(y)^{\frac{1}{r}}|^{q})^{\frac{p/r}{q/r}}dy)^{\frac{1/r}{p/r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\sum_{l=-\infty}^{\infty}|2^{ls}f_{l}(y)|^{q})^{\frac{p}{q}}dy)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

(163)

And by definition of the Peetre-Fefferman-Stein maximal function,
f_{l}(y)\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{l}(y)
for every
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
l\in\mathbb{Z}
thus

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\sum_{l=-\infty}^{\infty}|2^{ls}f_{l}(y)|^{q})^{\frac{p}{q}}dy)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\sum_{l=-\infty}^{\infty}|2^{ls}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{l}(y)|^{q})^{\frac{p}{q}}dy)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

(164)

Therefore we have reached the conclusion that for
s\in\mathbb{R},0<p,q<\infty
, the following is true

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle=(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\sum_{l=-\infty}^{\infty}|2^{ls}f_{l}(y)|^{q})^{\frac{p}{q}}dy)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\sim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\sum_{l=-\infty}^{\infty}|2^{ls}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{l}(y)|^{q})^{\frac{p}{q}}dy)^{\frac{1}{p}}.

(165)

Next, we provide useful lemmas in the proofs of our main theorems.

Lemma 2.14
.

Let
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
L\in\mathbb{N}
,
r>0
and for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and
j\in\mathbb{Z}
, if
h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
|h|\sim 2^{-k}
and
f_{j}=\psi_{2^{-j}}*f
, then we have the following two estimates

\displaystyle|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x-y)|\cdot(1+\frac{|y|}{|h|})^{\frac{-n}{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{(j-k)L}(1+2^{j-k})^{\frac{n}{r}}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}(x),

(166)

\displaystyle|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x-y)|\cdot(1+\frac{|y|}{|h|})^{\frac{-n}{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+2^{j-k})^{\frac{n}{r}}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}(x),

(167)

and constants are independent of
y
and
h
.

Proof.

To prove (
166
), we use the mean value theorem and the iteration formula (
13
