) consecutively and obtain

|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x-y)|\lesssim\sum_{|\alpha|=L}|\partial^{\alpha}f_{j}(x-y+\sum^{L}_{l=1}t_{\alpha,l}h)|\cdot|h|^{L},

(168)

where
\alpha
represents a multi-index and each
t_{\alpha,l}
is in the interval
(0,1)
. For each multi-index
\alpha
with
|\alpha|=L
and since
\mathcal{F}_{n}\partial^{\alpha}f_{j}(\xi)
is supported in
\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:2^{j-1}\leq|\xi|<2^{j+1}\}
, we use the basic inequality

(1+2^{j+1}|y-\sum^{L}_{l=1}t_{\alpha,l}h|)^{\frac{n}{r}}\lesssim(1+2^{j-k}\cdot 2^{k}|y|+2^{j}|\sum^{L}_{l=1}t_{\alpha,l}h|)^{\frac{n}{r}}\lesssim(1+2^{k}|y|)^{\frac{n}{r}}\cdot(1+2^{j-k})^{\frac{n}{r}}

(169)

and obtain that

\displaystyle|\partial^{\alpha}f_{j}(x-y+\sum^{L}_{l=1}t_{\alpha,l}h)|

\displaystyle=\frac{|\partial^{\alpha}f_{j}(x-y+\sum^{L}_{l=1}t_{\alpha,l}h)|}{(1+2^{j+1}|y-\sum^{L}_{l=1}t_{\alpha,l}h|)^{\frac{n}{r}}}\cdot(1+2^{j+1}|y-\sum^{L}_{l=1}t_{\alpha,l}h|)^{\frac{n}{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+2^{k}|y|)^{\frac{n}{r}}\cdot(1+2^{j-k})^{\frac{n}{r}}\mathcal{P}_{n}(\partial^{\alpha}f_{j})(x).

(170)

We also use Remark
2.8
to get
\mathcal{P}_{n}(\partial^{\alpha}f_{j})(x)\lesssim 2^{jL}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}(x)
. Since
|h|\sim 2^{-k}
, we also have

(1+2^{k}|y|)^{\frac{n}{r}}\lesssim(1+\frac{|y|}{|h|})^{\frac{n}{r}}.

We insert these estimates and (
2
) into (
168
) and obtain (
166
). To prove (
167
), we use (
14
), (
15
) and Remark
2.4
to get

|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x-y)|\lesssim\sum_{l=0}^{L}|f_{j}(x-y+lh)|\lesssim\sum_{l=0}^{L}(1+2^{j}|y-lh|)^{\frac{n}{r}}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}(x).

(171)

Since
|h|\sim 2^{-k}
, then

(1+2^{j}|y-lh|)^{\frac{n}{r}}\lesssim(1+2^{k}|y|)^{\frac{n}{r}}\cdot(1+2^{j-k})^{\frac{n}{r}}\lesssim(1+\frac{|y|}{|h|})^{\frac{n}{r}}\cdot(1+2^{j-k})^{\frac{n}{r}}.

(172)

Inserting (
172
) into (
171
) yields (
167
).
∎

Lemma 2.15
.

Let
f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,
\tau\in[1,2]
and
\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
satisfy the condition that for all
\xi\in spt.f
, there are positive real numbers
a
and
b
, which can be sufficiently small and are independent of
\tau
and
\theta
, such that
a\leq|\theta\cdot\xi|\leq b
. Assume
L
is a positive integer. Then for any positive integer
N
, there exists a positive constant
C
such that

|\mathcal{F}_{n}(\frac{f(\xi)}{(e^{2\pi i\tau\theta\cdot\xi}-1)^{L}})(x)|\leq\frac{C}{(1+|x|)^{N}}\qquad\text{for all }x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(173)

Most importantly, the constant
C
may depend on
f,L,N,a,b
but is independent of
\tau\in[1,2]
and
\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
.

Proof.

If
|x|<1
, we use Taylor expansion and obtain

(e^{2\pi i\tau\theta\cdot\xi}-1)^{L}=(2\pi i\tau\theta\cdot\xi)^{L}\cdot(1+O(2\pi i\tau\theta\cdot\xi)).

(174)

Since
\tau\in[1,2]
and if
\xi\in spt.f
,
a
and
b
are sufficiently small, then we have

|(e^{2\pi i\tau\theta\cdot\xi}-1)^{L}|\geq C_{1}a^{L}(1-C_{2}b)>0,

(175)

where
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
are constants independent of
\tau
and
\theta
. Hence we have

|\mathcal{F}_{n}(\frac{f(\xi)}{(e^{2\pi i\tau\theta\cdot\xi}-1)^{L}})(x)|\!\leq\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|f(\xi)|}{|(e^{2\pi i\tau\theta\cdot\xi}-1)^{L}|}d\xi\!\lesssim\!\|f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\!\lesssim\!\frac{C}{(1+|x|)^{N}}

(176)

if
|x|<1
and the constant
C
is independent of
\tau\in[1,2]
and
\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
.

If
|x|\geq 1
, without loss of generality we can assume that
x=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
|x_{1}|=\max\{|x_{1}|,|x_{2}|,\cdots,|x_{n}|\}>0
. Using integration by parts with respect to
\xi_{1}
, the condition that
f\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and the basic formula

e^{-2\pi ix_{1}\xi_{1}}=\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{1}}(\frac{e^{-2\pi ix_{1}\xi_{1}}}{-2\pi ix_{1}}),

we can obtain

\mathcal{F}_{n}(\frac{f(\xi)}{(e^{2\pi i\tau\theta\cdot\xi}-1)^{L}})(x)=\frac{1}{2\pi ix_{1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\xi_{1}}(\frac{f(\xi)}{(e^{2\pi i\tau\theta\cdot\xi}-1)^{L}})\cdot e^{-2\pi ix\cdot\xi}d\xi.

We can iterate the integration by parts with respect to
\xi_{1}
for
N
times and obtain

\mathcal{F}_{n}(\frac{f(\xi)}{(e^{2\pi i\tau\theta\cdot\xi}-1)^{L}})(x)=\frac{1}{(2\pi ix_{1})^{N}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{\partial^{N}}{\partial\xi_{1}^{N}}(\frac{f(\xi)}{(e^{2\pi i\tau\theta\cdot\xi}-1)^{L}})\cdot e^{-2\pi ix\cdot\xi}d\xi.

(177)

By direct calculation, we have

|\frac{\partial^{k}}{\partial\xi_{1}^{k}}(e^{2\pi i\tau\theta\cdot\xi}-1)^{-L}|\lesssim|\theta_{1}|^{k}\sum_{j=0}^{k}|(e^{2\pi i\tau\theta\cdot\xi}-1)^{-L-j}|\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{k}|(e^{2\pi i\tau\theta\cdot\xi}-1)^{-L-j}|

(178)

for every nonnegative integer
k
and the constants are independent of
\tau\in[1,2]
and
\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
. Furthermore, using (
174
) and (
175
), if
\xi\in spt.f
we can choose sufficiently small positive numbers
a
and
b
so that
a\leq|\theta\cdot\xi|\leq b
implies

|(e^{2\pi i\tau\theta\cdot\xi}-1)^{L+j}|\geq C_{3}>0\qquad\text{for all }0\leq j\leq N,

(179)

and
C_{3}
is independent of
\tau\in[1,2]
and
\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
. Therefore Leibniz rule, (
177
), (
178
) and (
179
) tell us that

|\mathcal{F}_{n}(\frac{f(\xi)}{(e^{2\pi i\tau\theta\cdot\xi}-1)^{L}})(x)|\lesssim\frac{1}{|x|^{N}}\sum_{k=0}^{N}\|\frac{\partial^{k}f}{\partial\xi_{1}^{k}}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\frac{C}{(1+|x|)^{N}}

(180)

if
|x|\geq 1
and the constants are independent of
\tau\in[1,2]
and
\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
. The proof of Lemma
2.15
is now complete.
∎

Lemma 2.16
.

Suppose
f
is a tempered distribution in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Recall that
f_{l}(x)=\psi_{2^{-l}}*f(x)
for every
l\in\mathbb{Z}
,
x=(x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Then for every fixed
x_{1}^{\prime}=(x_{2},\cdots,x_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
, the smooth function
x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}\mapsto f_{l}(x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime})
is an element in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})
and its distributional Fourier transform
\mathcal{F}_{1}f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})
is supported in the set
\{u\in\mathbb{R}:|u|\leq 2^{l+1}\}
and hence the associated
1
-dimensional Peetre-Fefferman-Stein maximal function can be defined as

\mathcal{P}_{1}f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})(u)=\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{z\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{|f_{l}(u-z,x_{1}^{\prime})|}{(1+2^{l+1}|z|)^{\frac{1}{r}}}.

(181)

Proof.

Since
f\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\psi_{2^{-l}}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, by Theorem 2.3.20 of
[
2
]
,
f_{l}
is a smooth function and there exist positive constants
a
and
b
such that

|f_{l}(x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime})|\leq a(1+|x_{1}|+|x_{1}^{\prime}|)^{b}\leq a(1+|x_{1}^{\prime}|)^{b}(1+|x_{1}|)^{b}.

This inequality shows for fixed
x_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
, the smooth function
x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}\mapsto f_{l}(x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime})
is in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})
. To prove the distributional Fourier transform
\mathcal{F}_{1}f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})
is supported in the set
\{u\in\mathbb{R}:|u|\leq 2^{l+1}\}
, we find a sequence
\{\varphi_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\subseteq C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
so that
\{\varphi_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
converges to
f
in the sense of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Next, we establish the equality

<\mathcal{F}_{1}f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime}),g>=\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}<\mathcal{F}_{1}(\psi_{2^{-l}}*\varphi_{k}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})),g>

(182)

for every
g\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R})
. With an argument like above, the smooth function
x_{1}\in\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\psi_{2^{-l}}*\varphi_{k}(x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime})
is an element of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R})
, thus we have the following

\displaystyle<\mathcal{F}_{1}(\psi_{2^{-l}}*\varphi_{k}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})),g>

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi_{2^{-l}}*\varphi_{k}(u,x_{1}^{\prime})\cdot\mathcal{F}_{1}g(u)du

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\varphi_{k}(y_{1},y_{1}^{\prime})\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi_{2^{-l}}(u-y_{1},x_{1}^{\prime}-y_{1}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{1}g(u)dudy_{1}dy_{1}^{\prime}.

(183)

It is not hard to check by direct calculations that for fixed
x_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
, the function
(y_{1},y_{1}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\mapsto\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi_{2^{-l}}(u-y_{1},x_{1}^{\prime}-y_{1}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{1}g(u)du
is an element of
\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Therefore we have

\displaystyle\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}<\mathcal{F}_{1}(\psi_{2^{-l}}*\varphi_{k}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})),g>

\displaystyle=\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}<\varphi_{k},\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi_{2^{-l}}(u-\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime}-\cdot)\mathcal{F}_{1}g(u)du>

\displaystyle=<f,\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi_{2^{-l}}(u-\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime}-\cdot)\mathcal{F}_{1}g(u)du>.

(184)

We now justify the equation

<f,\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi_{2^{-l}}(u-\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime}-\cdot)\mathcal{F}_{1}g(u)du\!>=\!\int_{\mathbb{R}}\!<\!f,\psi_{2^{-l}}(u-\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime}-\cdot)\!>\!\mathcal{F}_{1}g(u)du.

(185)

Since
f
is a tempered distribution in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, (
185
) requires the Riemann sums of the integral
\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi_{2^{-l}}(u-y_{1},x_{1}^{\prime}-y_{1}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{1}g(u)du
converges to that integral in Schwartz seminorms with respect to
y=(y_{1},y_{1}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. For a sufficiently large
N\in\mathbb{N}
, we consider the interval
[-N,N]
and decompose it into
2N^{2}
disjoint subintervals
\{I_{m}\}_{m=1}^{2N^{2}}
of equal length
\frac{1}{N}
and pick
u_{m}\in I_{m}
, then
[-N,N]=\bigcup_{m=1}^{2N^{2}}I_{m}
. The difference between the integral and its
N
-th Riemann sum is given by

\int_{\mathbb{R}}\psi_{2^{-l}}(u-y_{1},x_{1}^{\prime}-y_{1}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{1}g(u)du-\sum_{m=1}^{2N^{2}}|I_{m}|\cdot\psi_{2^{-l}}(u_{m}-y_{1},x_{1}^{\prime}-y_{1}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{1}g(u_{m})

(186)

and can be written as a sum of the following two terms,

\int_{|u|>N}\psi_{2^{-l}}(u-y_{1},x_{1}^{\prime}-y_{1}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{1}g(u)du

(187)

and

\sum_{m=1}^{2N^{2}}\int_{I_{m}}\psi_{2^{-l}}(u-y_{1},x_{1}^{\prime}-y_{1}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{1}g(u)-\psi_{2^{-l}}(u_{m}-y_{1},x_{1}^{\prime}-y_{1}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{1}g(u_{m})du.

(188)

It is sufficient to show both (
187
) and (
188
) converge to zero in Schwartz seminorms with respect to
y
as
N\rightarrow\infty
. Let
\alpha=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2},\cdots,\alpha_{n})=(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{1}^{\prime})
and
\beta=(\beta_{1},\beta_{2},\cdots,\beta_{n})
denote multi-indices. To estimate the Schwartz seminorms of (
187
), we compute as follows

\displaystyle|y^{\alpha}\cdot\partial_{y}^{\beta}(\int_{|u|>N}\psi_{2^{-l}}(u-y_{1},x_{1}^{\prime}-y_{1}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{1}g(u)du)|

\displaystyle\lesssim|y_{1}|^{\alpha_{1}}\cdot|y_{1}^{\prime}|^{|\alpha_{1}^{\prime}|}\cdot\int_{|u|>N}|(\partial^{\beta}\psi)(2^{l}u-2^{l}y_{1},2^{l}x_{1}^{\prime}-2^{l}y_{1}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{1}g(u)|du

\displaystyle\lesssim|y_{1}|^{\alpha_{1}}\cdot|y_{1}^{\prime}|^{|\alpha_{1}^{\prime}|}\cdot\int_{|u|>N}\frac{1}{(1+4^{l}|u-y_{1}|^{2}+4^{l}|x_{1}^{\prime}-y_{1}^{\prime}|^{2})^{2M}}\cdot\frac{1}{(1+u^{2})^{M}}du

\displaystyle\lesssim|y_{1}|^{\alpha_{1}}\cdot|y_{1}^{\prime}|^{|\alpha_{1}^{\prime}|}\cdot\int_{|u|>N}\frac{1}{(1+|u-y_{1}|^{2})^{M}(1+|x_{1}^{\prime}-y_{1}^{\prime}|^{2})^{M}(1+u^{2})^{M}}du,

(189)

for some arbitrarily large positive integer
M
. We apply the following estimates

(1+y_{1}^{2})^{\frac{M}{2}}\leq 2^{\frac{M}{2}}(1+|u-y_{1}|^{2})^{\frac{M}{2}}\cdot(1+u^{2})^{\frac{M}{2}},

(190)

\frac{1}{(1+|u-y_{1}|^{2})^{\frac{M}{2}}}\leq 1,\text{ and }|y_{1}^{\prime}|^{|\alpha_{1}^{\prime}|}\leq(|y_{1}^{\prime}-x_{1}^{\prime}|^{2}+|x_{1}^{\prime}|^{2})^{\frac{|\alpha_{1}^{\prime}|}{2}},

(191)

then we can estimate (
2
) from above by

\frac{|y_{1}|^{\alpha_{1}}\cdot|y_{1}^{\prime}|^{|\alpha_{1}^{\prime}|}}{(1+y_{1}^{2})^{\frac{M}{2}}(1+|x_{1}^{\prime}-y_{1}^{\prime}|^{2})^{M}}\cdot\int_{|u|>N}\frac{1}{(1+u^{2})^{\frac{M}{2}}}du,

(192)

where the constant may depend on
\alpha,\beta,l,\psi,g,M,x_{1}^{\prime}
. And (
192
) tends to zero as
N\rightarrow\infty
uniformly in
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
if we pick
M\in\mathbb{N}
to be sufficiently large. This shows the Schwartz seminorms of (
187
) with respect to
y
converge to zero as
N\rightarrow\infty
. To estimate the Schwartz seminorms of each term in the summation of (
188
), we use Mean Value Theorem with respect to
u
and obtain

\displaystyle|y^{\alpha}\cdot\partial_{y}^{\beta}(\int_{I_{m}}\psi_{2^{-l}}(u-y_{1},x_{1}^{\prime}-y_{1}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{1}g(u)-\psi_{2^{-l}}(u_{m}-y_{1},x_{1}^{\prime}-y_{1}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{1}g(u_{m})du)|

\displaystyle\lesssim|y^{\alpha}|\int_{I_{m}}|\frac{d}{du}[(\partial^{\beta}\psi)(2^{l}u-2^{l}y_{1},2^{l}x_{1}^{\prime}-2^{l}y_{1}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{1}g(u)]\bigg|_{u=t_{m}}|\cdot|u-u_{m}|du

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|y_{1}|^{\alpha_{1}}\cdot|y_{1}^{\prime}|^{|\alpha_{1}^{\prime}|}}{N}\int_{I_{m}}\frac{1}{(1+4^{l}|t_{m}-y_{1}|^{2}+4^{l}|x_{1}^{\prime}-y_{1}^{\prime}|^{2})^{2M}}\cdot\frac{1}{(1+t_{m}^{2})^{M}}du

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{|y_{1}|^{\alpha_{1}}\cdot|y_{1}^{\prime}|^{|\alpha_{1}^{\prime}|}}{N}\int_{I_{m}}\frac{1}{(1+|x_{1}^{\prime}-y_{1}^{\prime}|^{2})^{M}(1+|t_{m}-y_{1}|^{2})^{M}(1+t_{m}^{2})^{M}}du,

for some
t_{m}\in I_{m}
between
u
and
u_{m}
. When
N
is large, we have
|u-t_{m}|\leq\frac{1}{N}<\frac{1}{2}
and
u^{2}\leq 2t_{m}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}
. We also use the estimate
(1+y_{1}^{2})^{\frac{M}{2}}\leq 2^{\frac{M}{2}}(1+|t_{m}-y_{1}|^{2})^{\frac{M}{2}}\cdot(1+t_{m}^{2})^{\frac{M}{2}}
and then we can dominate the above inequality by

\frac{|y_{1}|^{\alpha_{1}}\cdot|y_{1}^{\prime}|^{|\alpha_{1}^{\prime}|}}{N(1+y_{1}^{2})^{\frac{M}{2}}(1+|x_{1}^{\prime}-y_{1}^{\prime}|^{2})^{M}}\cdot\int_{I_{m}}\frac{1}{(1+u^{2})^{\frac{M}{2}}}du.

(193)

Summing over
m=1,\cdots,2N^{2}
and taking supremum over
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
yield that the Schwartz seminorms of (
188
) with respect to
y
can be estimated from above by

\frac{1}{N}\int_{[-N,N]}\frac{1}{(1+u^{2})^{\frac{M}{2}}}du,

(194)

where
M
is a sufficiently large positive integer and the constant may depend on
\alpha,\beta,l,\psi,g,M,x_{1}^{\prime}
. And (
194
) tends to zero as
N\rightarrow\infty
. Therefore the validity of equation (
185
) has been proved. Furthermore from equation
(2.3.21)
on page 127 of
[
2
]
, we know
<f,\psi_{2^{-l}}(u-\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime}-\cdot)>
can be identified with the function
f_{l}(u,x_{1}^{\prime})
. By inserting this identification into (
185
) and combining the result with (
2
), we have obtained

<\mathcal{F}_{1}f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime}),g>=<f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime}),\mathcal{F}_{1}g>=\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}<\mathcal{F}_{1}(\psi_{2^{-l}}*\varphi_{k}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})),g>.

(195)

Since
\psi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\varphi_{k}\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, we have

\psi_{2^{-l}}*\varphi_{k}(x)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{1}[\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n-1}[\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{-l}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}\varphi_{k}(\xi)](x_{1}^{\prime})](x_{1}),

(196)

where
\xi=(\xi_{1},\xi_{1}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and the
(n-1)
-dimensional inverse Fourier transform is done with respect to
\xi_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
and the
1
-dimensional inverse Fourier transform is done with respect to
\xi_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
. Hence for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle<\mathcal{F}_{1}(\psi_{2^{-l}}*\varphi_{k}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})),g>

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}_{1}(\psi_{2^{-l}}*\varphi_{k}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime}))(u)\cdot g(u)du

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n-1}[\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{-l}u,2^{-l}\xi_{1}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{n}\varphi_{k}(u,\xi_{1}^{\prime})](x_{1}^{\prime})\cdot g(u)du

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n-1}[\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{-l}u,2^{-l}\xi_{1}^{\prime})\mathcal{F}_{n}\varphi_{k}(u,\xi_{1}^{\prime})g(u)du](x_{1}^{\prime}),

(197)

and from (
2
) we also see that if
spt.g
is contained in the complement of the set
\{u\in\mathbb{R}:|u|\leq 2^{l+1}\}
, then
<\mathcal{F}_{1}(\psi_{2^{-l}}*\varphi_{k}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})),g>=0
for every
k\in\mathbb{N}
. Therefore the distributional Fourier transform
\mathcal{F}_{1}f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})
is supported in the set
\{u\in\mathbb{R}:|u|\leq 2^{l+1}\}
and the proof of Lemma
2.16
is now concluded.
∎

3.
Triebel-Lizorkin space and maximal functions of means of differences

Proof Of Theorem
1.4
.

In this proof we consider the number
r
appearing in Definition
1.3
equals the number
r
appearing in Definition
2.2
. We first prove that

\|\{2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f(x)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

(198)

when
0<p<\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
,
\frac{n}{\min\{p,q\}}<s<L
and for every
r\in\mathbb{R}
with
\frac{n}{s}<r<\min\{p,q\}
. We denote

S_{k}^{*}f(x)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq\tau<2\\
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\end{subarray}}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0pt\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}|\varDelta^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}z}f_{j}(x-y)|\cdot(1+2^{k}|y|)^{\frac{-n}{r}}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(z).

(199)

Since
|\tau 2^{-k}z|\sim 2^{-k}
if
1\leq\tau<2
and
z\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
, then we have

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0pt\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}|\varDelta^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}z}f_{j}(x-y)|\cdot(1+2^{k}|y|)^{\frac{-n}{r}}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(z)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0pt\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}|\varDelta^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}z}f_{j}(x-y)|\cdot(1+\frac{|y|}{|\tau 2^{-k}z|})^{\frac{-n}{r}}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(z).

We use (
166
) for
j\leq k
and (
167
) for
j>k
, and obtain

\displaystyle S_{k}^{*}f(x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j\leq k}2^{(j-k)L}(1+2^{j-k})^{\frac{n}{r}}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}(x)+\sum_{j>k}(1+2^{j-k})^{\frac{n}{r}}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}(x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j\leq k}2^{(j-k)L}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}(x)+\sum_{j>k}2^{(j-k)\frac{n}{r}}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}(x).

(200)

For
0<q<\infty
, we pick
0<\varepsilon<\min\{L-s,s-\frac{n}{r}\}
and deduce from (
3
) the following

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}(S_{k}^{*}f(x))^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}(\sum_{j\leq k}2^{j\varepsilon}\cdot 2^{-j\varepsilon+(j-k)L}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}(x))^{q}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}(\sum_{j>k}2^{-j\varepsilon}\cdot 2^{j\varepsilon+(j-k)\frac{n}{r}}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}(x))^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}(\sum_{j\leq k}2^{j\varepsilon})^{q}\cdot\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\leq k}2^{-lq\varepsilon+(l-k)qL}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{l}(x)^{q}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}(\sum_{j>k}2^{-j\varepsilon})^{q}\cdot\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l>k}2^{lq\varepsilon+(l-k)q\frac{n}{r}}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{l}(x)^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{kq(s+\varepsilon)}\sum_{l\leq k}2^{-lq\varepsilon+(l-k)qL}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{l}(x)^{q}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{kq(s-\varepsilon)}\sum_{l>k}2^{lq\varepsilon+(l-k)q\frac{n}{r}}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{l}(x)^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{lsq}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{l}(x)^{q},

(201)

where in the last inequality we switched the order of summation. Then we raise the power to
\frac{1}{q}
, apply
\|\cdot\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
-quasinorm to both sides of (
3
), use Remark
2.13
and we can obtain the estimate

\|\{2^{ks}S_{k}^{*}f(x)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{jsq}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}(x)^{q})^{\frac{p}{q}}dx)^{\frac{1}{p}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(202)

If
q=\infty
, we use the same
\varepsilon
as above and obtain

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ks}S_{k}^{*}f(x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ks}\sum_{j\leq k}2^{j\varepsilon}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\leq k}2^{-l\varepsilon+(l-k)L}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{l}(x)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ks}\sum_{j>k}2^{-j\varepsilon}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l>k}2^{l\varepsilon+(l-k)\frac{n}{r}}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{l}(x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(s+\varepsilon)}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\leq k}2^{-l\varepsilon+(l-k)L}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{l}(x)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(s-\varepsilon)}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l>k}2^{l\varepsilon+(l-k)\frac{n}{r}}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{l}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\geq l}2^{k(s+\varepsilon-L)}\cdot 2^{l(L-\varepsilon)}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{l}(x)

\displaystyle\quad+\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k<l}2^{k(s-\varepsilon-\frac{n}{r})}\cdot 2^{l(\frac{n}{r}+\varepsilon)}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{l}(x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ls}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{l}(x).

(203)

We apply
\|\cdot\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
-quasinorm to both sides of (
3
), use Remark
2.7
and the mapping property of Hardy-Littlewood maximal function to get

\displaystyle\|\{2^{ks}S_{k}^{*}f(x)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{\infty})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ls}\mathcal{M}_{n}(|f_{l}|^{r})(x)^{\frac{1}{r}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{lsr}|f_{l}|^{r})(x)\|_{L^{\frac{p}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{\frac{1}{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ls}|f_{l}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(204)

The above proof also shows for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
,

\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{\begin{subarray}{c}1\leq\tau<2\\
y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\end{subarray}}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0pt\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}|\varDelta^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}z}f_{j}(x-y)|d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(z)\cdot(1+2^{k}|y|)^{\frac{-n}{r}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}<\infty,

(205)

and thus
\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}|\varDelta^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}z}f_{j}(x-y)|<\infty
for almost every
1\leq\tau<2
,
z\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
,
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Therefore we can infer from (
20
) that

\varDelta^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}z}f(x-y)=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\varDelta^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}z}f_{j}(x-y)

(206)

in the sense of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})/\mathscr{P}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, and almost every
1\leq\tau<2
,
z\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
,
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. The above justification of decomposition also tells us that

\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f(x)\lesssim S_{k}^{*}f(x),

and this estimate, combined with (
202
) and (
3
), finishes the proof of (
198
). We also observe that for
0<q<\infty

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}\big(\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{0<\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f(x)\big)^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{j\geq 0}2^{ksq}\big(\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{2^{-j}\leq\tau<2^{1-j}}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f(x)\big)^{q}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geq 0}2^{-jsq}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{(k+j)sq}\big(\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k-j}}f(x)\big)^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}\big(\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f(x)\big)^{q},

(207)

and for
q=\infty

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{0<\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f(x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{j\geq 0}2^{-js}\cdot 2^{(k+j)s}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k-j}}f(x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\big(\sum_{j\geq 0}2^{-js}\big)\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f(x),

(208)

therefore (
26
) can be estimated from above by
\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
. Using the same method, we can also estimate (
28
) from above by
\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
under the conditions of Theorem
1.4
. As for (
29
), by using the same method as (
198
) we can show that

\|\{2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq|h|<2}D^{L}_{2^{-k}h}f(x)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

(209)

when
p,q,s,r,L
satisfy conditions of Theorem
1.4
. And then we use the arguments in (
3
) and (
3
) to prove that

\|\{2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{0<|h|<2}D^{L}_{2^{-k}h}f(x)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}\lesssim\|\{2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq|h|<2}D^{L}_{2^{-k}h}f(x)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}

(210)

for all
0<p<\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
, since we have the decomposition

\{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:0<|h|<2\}=\bigcup_{j=0}^{\infty}\{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:2^{-j}\leq|h|<2^{1-j}\}.

To prove the reverse directions, we first show that for any
0<\tau<2
,
0<p<\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, we have the estimate

\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|\{2^{ks}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f(x)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}.

(211)

Let
\tau\in(0,2)
be fixed for now, and let
a
denote the tempered distribution in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
whose distributional Fourier transform is the function below

\mathcal{F}_{n}a(\xi)=\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0pt\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}(e^{2\pi i\tau\xi\cdot z}-1)^{L}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(z).

(212)

For example, we can choose
a=\sum_{m=0}^{L}\binom{L}{m}(-1)^{L-m}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0pt\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}}\delta_{-m\tau z}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(z)
, where
\delta_{-m\tau z}
is the Dirac mass at
-m\tau z
. Then from (
19
) and Definition
1.3
we deduce the following equality

S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f(x)=\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{n}a(2^{-k}\xi)\cdot\mathcal{F}_{n}f(\xi))(x-y)|}{(1+\tau^{-1}2^{k}|y|)^{\frac{n}{r}}}.

(213)

Using the formula given in Appendix D.3 of
[
2
]
, we have

\mathcal{F}_{n}a(\xi)=C_{n}\cdot\int_{-1}^{1}(e^{2\pi it\tau|\xi|}-1)^{L}(1-t^{2})^{\frac{n-3}{2}}dt,

(214)

where
C_{n}
is a positive constant depending on
n
. By using Taylor expansion, we can write

(e^{2\pi it\tau|\xi|}-1)^{L}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}A_{L+k}(t\tau|\xi|)^{L+k},

(215)

and each
A_{L+k}
is a complex number whose value is independent of
t,\tau,\xi
and satisfies
|A_{L+k}|>0
. Hence we have the expression

\mathcal{F}_{n}a(\xi)=C_{n}\cdot\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}A_{L+k}B_{L+k}|\tau\xi|^{L+k}\text{ for every $\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}$ when $|\xi|$ is small,}

(216)

where

B_{L+k}=\int_{-1}^{1}t^{L+k}(1-t^{2})^{\frac{n-3}{2}}dt\qquad\text{for }k\geq 0,

(217)

and
B_{L+k}=0
if
L+k
is an odd integer,
B_{L+k}>0
if
L+k
is an even integer. If
L
is an even integer, then

\mathcal{F}_{n}a(\xi)=C_{n}A_{L}B_{L}|\tau\xi|^{L}(1+O(|\tau\xi|^{2}))

and
|\mathcal{F}_{n}a(\xi)|\sim|\xi|^{L}>0
if
|\xi|>0
is sufficiently small. If
L
is an odd integer, then

\mathcal{F}_{n}a(\xi)=C_{n}A_{L+1}B_{L+1}|\tau\xi|^{L+1}(1+O(|\tau\xi|^{2}))

and
|\mathcal{F}_{n}a(\xi)|\sim|\xi|^{L+1}>0
if
|\xi|>0
is sufficiently small. Therefore we can pick a sufficiently large positive integer
m_{1}
so that
|\mathcal{F}_{n}a(\xi)|>0
if
0<|\xi|<2^{1-m_{1}}
, and hence
\frac{\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{1}}\xi)}{\mathcal{F}_{n}a(\xi)}
is a well-defined function in
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\frac{\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{1}}\xi)}{\mathcal{F}_{n}a(\xi)})(\cdot)\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Furthermore by using (
213
) we have for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}

\displaystyle|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{1}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f(\xi))(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\frac{\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{1}-k}\xi)}{\mathcal{F}_{n}a(2^{-k}\xi)})(y)|\cdot|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{n}a(2^{-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f(\xi))(x-y)|dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\frac{\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{1}}\xi)}{\mathcal{F}_{n}a(\xi)})(2^{k}y)|\cdot 2^{kn}(1+\tau^{-1}2^{k}|y|)^{\frac{n}{r}}dy\cdot S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f(x)

\displaystyle\lesssim S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f(x),

(218)

and the constants are independent of
k\in\mathbb{Z}
. By using (
3
) above, we reach the conclusion that for
0<p<\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle=2^{-sm_{1}}\|\{2^{ks}\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{1}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f(\xi))(x)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\{2^{ks}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f(x)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}.

(219)

We let
\tau=1
in (
3
) and get that
\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
can be estimated from above by (
25
). Therefore we have shown that (
25
) and (
26
) are equivalent quasinorms in
\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
when parameters
p,q,s,r,L
satisfy the conditions of Theorem
1.4
.

To show that when
0<p<\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, the quasinorm
\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
can be estimated from above by (
27
), we consider the tempered distribution
b\in\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
whose distributional Fourier transform is the function below

\mathcal{F}_{n}b(\xi)=\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0ptA}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A}}(e^{2\pi i\xi\cdot z}-1)^{L}dz=\frac{1}{|A|}\int_{1}^{2}\tau^{n-1}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}(e^{2\pi i\tau\xi\cdot z}-1)^{L}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(z)d\tau.

(220)

For example, we can choose
b=\sum_{m=0}^{L}\binom{L}{m}(-1)^{L-m}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0ptA}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A}}\delta_{-mz}dz
, where
\delta_{-mz}
is the Dirac mass at
-mz
. Using (
212
), (
214
), (
215
) and (
216
), we obtain that

\mathcal{F}_{n}b(\xi)=C^{\prime}_{n}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\int_{1}^{2}\tau^{n+k+L-1}d\tau\cdot A_{L+k}B_{L+k}|\xi|^{L+k}=C^{\prime}_{n}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}A^{\prime}_{L+k}B_{L+k}|\xi|^{L+k}

(221)

for every
\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
when
|\xi|
is small, where
C^{\prime}_{n}
is a positive constant depending on
n
, each
A^{\prime}_{L+k}
is a complex number satisfying
|A^{\prime}_{L+k}|>0
, and each
B_{L+k}
is defined by (
217
). Therefore using a similar analysis like the one for
\mathcal{F}_{n}a(\xi)
, we can find a sufficiently large positive integer
m_{2}
so that
|\mathcal{F}_{n}b(\xi)|>0
if
0<|\xi|<2^{1-m_{2}}
. Hence
\frac{\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{2}}\xi)}{\mathcal{F}_{n}b(\xi)}
is a well-defined function in
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\frac{\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{2}}\xi)}{\mathcal{F}_{n}b(\xi)})(\cdot)\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Using a similar argument like the one to deduce (
3
) and the estimate

|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{n}b(2^{-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f(\xi))(x\!-\!y)|\!=\!|\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0ptA}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A}}\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}z}f(x\!-\!y)dz|\!\leq\!(1\!+\!2^{k}|y|)^{\frac{n}{r}}\cdot V^{L}_{2^{-k}}f(x),

(222)

we can obtain

|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{2}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f(\xi))(x)|\lesssim V^{L}_{2^{-k}}f(x)\qquad\text{for every }k\in\mathbb{Z},

(223)

and the constant is independent of
k
. By using (
223
) above, we reach the conclusion that for
0<p<\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle=2^{-sm_{2}}\|\{2^{ks}\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{2}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f(\xi))(x)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\{2^{ks}V^{L}_{2^{-k}}f(x)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}.

(224)

By using their defining expressions in Definition
1.3
, it is easy to see that
V^{L}_{2^{-k}}f(x)\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{0<|h|<2}D^{L}_{2^{-k}h}f(x)
for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, thus
\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
can be estimated from above by (
29
) for all
0<p<\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
. Hereby we conclude the proof of Theorem
1.4
.
∎

4.
Besov-Lipschitz space and maximal functions of means of differences

Proof Of Theorem
1.5
.

The proof of Theorem
1.5
is alike to the proof of Theorem
1.4
and thus we will just sketch it. In this proof we still consider the number
r
appearing in Definition
1.3
equals the number
r
appearing in Definition
2.2
. We first prove the counterpart of (
198
), that is,

\|\{2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

(225)

when
0<p\leq\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
,
\frac{n}{p}<s<L
and for every
r\in\mathbb{R}
with
\frac{n}{s}<r<p
. By using Lemma
2.14
, we still have (
3
) with
S_{k}^{*}f(x)
given in (
199
). If
1\leq p\leq\infty
, we use Minkowski’s inequality for
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-norms, Remark
2.7
and the mapping property of Hardy-Littlewood maximal function in a sequence and obtain the following

\|S_{k}^{*}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j\leq k}2^{(j-k)L}\|f_{j}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}+\sum_{j>k}2^{(j-k)\frac{n}{r}}\|f_{j}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(226)

With (
226
), we use the calculation method of (
3
) when
0<q<\infty
and the calculation method of (
3
) when
q=\infty
and justify the decomposition in a similar way like (
206
), then we can obtain (
225
) for the case
1\leq p\leq\infty
. If
0<p<1
, we raise the power of both sides of (
3
) to
p
and integrate over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
with respect to
x
, use Remark
2.7
and the mapping property of Hardy-Littlewood maximal function in a sequence and obtain the following

\|S_{k}^{*}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{p}\lesssim\sum_{j\leq k}2^{(j-k)Lp}\|f_{j}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{p}+\sum_{j>k}2^{(j-k)\frac{np}{r}}\|f_{j}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{p}.

(227)

With (
227
), we use the calculation method of (
3
) when
0<\frac{q}{p}<\infty
and the calculation method of (
3
) when
\frac{q}{p}=\infty
and justify the decomposition then we can obtain (
225
) for the case
0<p<1
. Next, we show that

\|\{2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{0<\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})}\lesssim\|\{2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})}

(228)

for
0<p\leq\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
. We have the following pointwise estimate

\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{0<\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f(x)\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k-j}}f(x)\qquad\text{for every }x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}.

(229)

If
1\leq p\leq\infty
, then we use Minkowski’s inequality for
L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-norms and get

\|\{2^{ks}\!\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{0<\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})}\!\lesssim\!\|\{2^{ks}\!\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k-j}}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}}.

(230)

When
0<q<1
, we can switch the order of summation and obtain

\displaystyle(\ref{eq401})

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k-j}}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle=(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-jsq}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{(k+j)sq}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k-j}}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\{2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})}.

(231)

When
1\leq q\leq\infty
, we use Minkowski’s inequality for
l^{q}
-norms and obtain

\displaystyle(\ref{eq401})

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-js}\|\{2^{(k+j)s}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k-j}}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\{2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})}.

(232)

If
0<p<1
, then we raise the power of both sides of (
229
) to
p
and integrate over
\mathbb{R}^{n}
with respect to
x
to obtain

\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{0<\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{p}\lesssim\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k-j}}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{p}.

(233)

When
0<\frac{q}{p}<1
, we use (
233
) to obtain

\displaystyle\|\{2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{0<\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{ksp}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k-j}}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{p})^{\frac{q}{p}})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{ksq}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k-j}}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle=(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-jsq}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{(k+j)sq}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k-j}}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\{2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})}.

(234)

When
1\leq\frac{q}{p}\leq\infty
, we use (
233
) and Minkowski’s inequality for
l^{\frac{q}{p}}
-norms and obtain

\displaystyle\|\{2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{0<\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\{\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{ksp}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k-j}}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{p}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{\frac{q}{p}}}^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}2^{-jsp}\|\{2^{(k+j)s}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k-j}}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}}^{p})^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\{2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{1\leq\tau<2}S^{L}_{\tau 2^{-k}}f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})}.

(235)

From (
230
), (
4
), (
4
), (
4
) and (
4
), we see that (
228
) has been proved. Combining (
225
) and (
228
) gives that (
31
) can be estimated from above by
\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
when conditions of Theorem
1.5
are satisfied. Using the same method, we also prove that (
33
) and (
34
) can be estimated from above by
\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
under the conditions of Theorem
1.5
.

To prove the reverse directions, we just notice that (
3
) and (
223
) are pointwise estimates for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and then we use the same method given in (
3
) and (
3
) to prove that
\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
can be estimated from above by (
30
) and (
32
) for all
0<p\leq\infty
,
0<q\leq\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
. Furthermore (
32
) can be estimated from above by (
34
) by using Definition
1.3
. The proof of Theorem
1.5
is complete.
∎

5.
Triebel-Lizorkin space and iterated differences

Proof Of Theorem
1.6
.

We first prove Theorem
1.6
(i). Let
f\in\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
be an element of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. We note that
\tilde{\sigma}_{pq}<s
implies
\frac{nq}{n+sq}<p
. And we recall the notation
A_{k}=\{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:2^{-k}\leq|h|<2^{1-k}\}
for
k\in\mathbb{Z}
. For
|h|\lesssim 2^{-k}
and
f_{j}=\psi_{2^{-j}}*f
, we deduce two estimates for
|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|
. Using mean value theorem and the iteration formula (
13
) consecutively, we get

|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|\lesssim\sum_{|\alpha|=L}|\partial^{\alpha}f_{j}(x+\sum^{L}_{l=1}t_{\alpha,l}h)|\cdot|h|^{L},

(236)

where
\alpha
represents a multi-index and each
t_{\alpha,l}
is in
(0,1)
. Since the
n
-dimensional distributional Fourier transform
\mathcal{F}_{n}f_{j}
is supported in
\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:2^{j-1}\leq|\xi|<2^{j+1}\}
, we use Remark
2.8
to get

|\partial^{\alpha}f_{j}(x+\sum^{L}_{l=1}t_{\alpha,l}h)|\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{n}(\partial^{\alpha}f_{j})(x+\sum^{L}_{l=1}t_{\alpha,l}h)\lesssim 2^{jL}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}(x+\sum^{L}_{l=1}t_{\alpha,l}h).

(237)

Since
|\sum^{L}_{l=1}t_{\alpha,l}h|\lesssim L2^{-k}
, by (
146
) of Remark
2.4
we have

\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}(x+\sum^{L}_{l=1}t_{\alpha,l}h)\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}(x)\cdot(1+L2^{j-k})^{n/r},

(238)

where
r
is the chosen positive number in Definition
2.2
and satisfies
0<r<\min\{p,q\}
. We infer from (
236
), (
237
) and (
238
) the first estimate

|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|\lesssim 2^{(j-k)L}(1+L2^{j-k})^{n/r}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}(x)\quad\text{for}\quad|h|\lesssim 2^{-k},

(239)

and the constant is independent of
h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
j,k\in\mathbb{Z}
. Also by using (
14
), we get

|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|\lesssim\sum^{L}_{l=0}|f_{j}(x+lh)|.

(240)

If
0\leq l\leq L
,
|h|\lesssim 2^{-k}
and
j>k
, we recall that
0<r<\min\{p,q\}
and the value of
r
will be determined later, then using Remark
2.4
, Lemma
2.6
and the definition of Peetre-Fefferman-Stein maximal function, we obtain

\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}(x+lh)\lesssim(1+2^{j+1}l|h|)^{n/r}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}(x)\lesssim 2^{(j-k)n/r}\mathcal{M}_{n}(|f_{j}|^{r})(x)^{1/r},

(241)

and the constant in (
241
) is independent of
h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
0\leq l\leq L
and
j,k\in\mathbb{Z}
. Using the proper change of variable, we also have

2^{kn}\int_{A_{k}}|f_{j}(x+lh)|^{r}dh\lesssim\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0ptl2^{-k}\leq|y|<l2^{-k+1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{l2^{-k}\leq|y|<l2^{-k+1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{l2^{-k}\leq|y|<l2^{-k+1}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{l2^{-k}\leq|y|<l2^{-k+1}}}|f_{j}(x+y)|^{r}dy\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{n}(|f_{j}|^{r})(x)

(242)

for
0<l\leq L
, and
|f_{j}(x)|^{r}\leq\mathcal{M}_{n}(|f_{j}|^{r})(x)
by Lebesgue’s differentiation theorem. Applying (
241
) and (
242
), we can obtain the second estimate

\displaystyle 2^{kn}\int_{A_{k}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|^{q}dh

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l=0}^{L}2^{kn}\int_{A_{k}}|f_{j}(x+lh)|^{r}\cdot|f_{j}(x+lh)|^{q-r}dh

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l=0}^{L}2^{kn}\int_{A_{k}}|f_{j}(x+lh)|^{r}dh\cdot\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}(x+lh)^{q-r}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{(j-k)n(\frac{q}{r}-1)}\mathcal{M}_{n}(|f_{j}|^{r})(x)^{\frac{q}{r}}.

(243)

And estimate (
5
) is true for
0<q<\infty
and
j>k
. Now we consider estimating the expression below

(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq+n)}\int_{A_{k}}(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|)^{q}dh)^{\frac{1}{q}}

(244)

by the sum of the following two expressions,

(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq+n)}\int_{A_{k}}(\sum_{j\leq k}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|)^{q}dh)^{\frac{1}{q}}

(245)

and

(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq+n)}\int_{A_{k}}(\sum_{j>k}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|)^{q}dh)^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(246)

For
0<q<\infty
, we pick
0<\varepsilon<\min\{s,L-s\}
and the value of
\varepsilon
will be determined later. Then we have

\displaystyle(\sum_{j\leq k}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|)^{q}

\displaystyle=(\sum_{j\leq k}2^{j\varepsilon}\cdot 2^{-j\varepsilon}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|)^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{j\leq k}2^{j\varepsilon})^{q}\cdot\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{j\leq k}2^{-jq\varepsilon}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{kq\varepsilon}\cdot\sum_{j\leq k}2^{-jq\varepsilon}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|^{q},

(247)

and

\displaystyle(\sum_{j>k}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|)^{q}

\displaystyle=(\sum_{j>k}2^{-j\varepsilon}\cdot 2^{j\varepsilon}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|)^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{j>k}2^{-j\varepsilon})^{q}\cdot\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{j>k}2^{jq\varepsilon}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-kq\varepsilon}\cdot\sum_{j>k}2^{jq\varepsilon}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|^{q}.

(248)

Using (
5
) and (
239
), we can estimate (
245
) from above by

\displaystyle(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{j\leq k}2^{k(n+qs+q\varepsilon)}2^{-jq\varepsilon}\int_{A_{k}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|^{q}dh)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{j\leq k}2^{k(qs+q\varepsilon)}2^{-jq\varepsilon}2^{(j-k)Lq}(1+L2^{j-k})^{\frac{nq}{r}}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}(x)^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(249)

We notice that
(1+L2^{j-k})^{nq/r}\lesssim C
if
j\leq k
and
C
is a constant determined by
n,q,r,L
and we switch the order of summation to obtain

\displaystyle(\ref{eq278})

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{k\geq j}2^{kq(s+\varepsilon-L)}2^{-jq\varepsilon+jqL}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}(x)^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle=(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{jqs}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}(x)^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{jqs}\mathcal{M}_{n}(|f_{j}|^{r})(x)^{\frac{q}{r}})^{\frac{1}{q}},

(250)

where we also used Remark
2.7
and the condition that
\varepsilon<L-s
. Using (
5
) and (
5
) and switching the order of summation, we can estimate (
246
) from above by

\displaystyle(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{j>k}2^{k(n+qs-q\varepsilon)}2^{jq\varepsilon}\int_{A_{k}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|^{q}dh)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{j>k}2^{kq(s-\varepsilon)+jq\varepsilon}\cdot 2^{(j-k)n(\frac{q}{r}-1)}\mathcal{M}_{n}(|f_{j}|^{r})(x)^{\frac{q}{r}})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{k<j}2^{kq[s-\varepsilon-n(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{q})]}\cdot 2^{jq[\varepsilon+n(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{q})]}\mathcal{M}_{n}(|f_{j}|^{r})(x)^{\frac{q}{r}})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(251)

If
q\leq p<\infty
, we have

\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0,r\rightarrow q}s-\varepsilon-n(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{q})=s>0.

If
\frac{nq}{n+sq}<p<q
, we have

\lim_{\varepsilon\rightarrow 0,r\rightarrow p}s-\varepsilon-n(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{q})=s-n(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q})>0.

Therefore if we pick
\varepsilon
sufficiently small and
r
sufficiently close to
\min\{p,q\}
, then we can make
s-\varepsilon-n(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{q})
a positive finite number and hence

\sum_{k<j}2^{kq[s-\varepsilon-n(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{q})]}\lesssim 2^{jq[s-\varepsilon-n(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{q})]}.

(252)

Inserting (
252
) into (
5
) yields

(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq+n)}\int_{A_{k}}(\sum_{j>k}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|)^{q}dh)^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{jqs}\mathcal{M}_{n}(|f_{j}|^{r})(x)^{\frac{q}{r}})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(253)

Combining (
245
), (
5
), (
5
), (
246
) and (
253
) and also invoking Lemma
2.12
, we can obtain

\|(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq+n)}\int_{A_{k}}(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|)^{q}dh)^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

(254)

when
0<p,q<\infty
and
\tilde{\sigma}_{pq}<s<L
. From the assumption
f\in\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
, we know inequality (
254
) also shows
\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|<\infty
for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and for almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n},h\in A_{k}
. Together with (
20
), we have reached the conclusion that

\varDelta^{L}_{h}f=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j}(x)\text{ in the sense of }\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})/\mathscr{P}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

(255)

for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
h\in A_{k},x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and the tempered distribution
\varDelta^{L}_{h}f
has a function representative which is the pointwise limit of the series
\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j}(x)
. Furthermore, integration of
\varDelta^{L}_{h}f
with respect to the Lebesgue measure is justified, and the inequality

\displaystyle\|(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-sq}|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f|^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq+n)}\int_{A_{k}}(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|)^{q}dh)^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

(256)

is also validated. Therefore the proof of Theorem
1.6
(i) is now complete.

Now we prove Theorem
1.6
(ii) when
f
is a function,
0<p<\infty
,
0<q<1
and
\sigma_{pq}+\tilde{\sigma}_{pq}<s<\infty
. Without loss of generality, we also assume the right side of (
37
) is finite, otherwise inequality (
37
) is trivial. To do this, recall that
spt.\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi\subseteq A^{\prime}=\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\frac{1}{2}\leq|\xi|<2\}
and by Taylor expansion of
e^{2\pi it}
, we have

(e^{2\pi it}-1)^{L}=(2\pi it)^{L}(1+O(2\pi it))

(257)

and there exists a sufficiently large positive integer
m_{0}
such that

0<|t|<2^{2-m_{0}}\text{ implies }|(e^{2\pi it}-1)^{L}|>0.

(258)

For a unit vector
\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
, we can find
\delta>0
so small that if
\xi\in A^{\prime}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
\frac{1}{4}\leq|\theta\cdot\xi|<2
, then for all other
\theta^{\prime}
in the spherical cap
C_{\theta}:=\{\theta^{\prime}\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}:|\theta^{\prime}-\theta|<\delta\}
, we also have
\frac{1}{4}\leq|\theta^{\prime}\cdot\xi|<2
. We choose properly distributed unit vectors
\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\cdots,\theta_{M}
where
M\in\mathbb{N}
is sufficiently large so that the spherical caps
C_{1},C_{2},\cdots,C_{M}
, respectively associated with
\theta_{1},\theta_{2},\cdots,\theta_{M}
in the above way, cover the unit sphere
\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
. For each cap
C_{l}
,
1\leq l\leq M
, we consider the set

P_{l}:=\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\frac{1}{2}\leq|\xi|<2,\frac{\xi}{|\xi|}\in C_{l}\},

(259)

then from the construction of
\{C_{l}\}_{l=1}^{M}
, we have that

\frac{1}{4}\leq|\theta\cdot\xi|<2\qquad\text{for all }\xi\in P_{l}\text{ and }\theta\in C_{l}

(260)

and

\bigcup_{l=1}^{M}P_{l}=A^{\prime}.

(261)

We use a partition of unity associated with
\{P_{l}\}_{l=1}^{M}
by smooth functions
\{\rho_{l}\}_{l=1}^{M}
with compact supports and
\{\rho_{l}\}_{l=1}^{M}
also satisfy

\sum_{l=1}^{M}\rho_{l}(\xi)=1\text{ if }\xi\in A^{\prime}\qquad\text{and}\qquad spt.\rho_{l}\bigcap A^{\prime}\subseteq P_{l}\text{ for each }l.

(262)

Recall the definition of
\mathcal{F}_{n}\phi
given in (
3
), we pick a large positive integer
J>m_{0}
and the value of
J
will be determined later, then we have for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}

\mathcal{F}_{n}\phi(2^{m_{0}-J-k}\xi)=1\qquad\text{if}\qquad|\xi|\leq 2^{k+J-m_{0}},

(263)

and by (
5
),

\mathcal{F}_{n}\phi(2^{m_{0}-J-k}\xi)=1-\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-J-k-j}\xi)=1-\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k-j}\xi).

(264)

Furthermore if
\tau\in[1,2]
,
\theta\in C_{l}
,
2^{m_{0}-k}\xi\in spt.\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi\bigcap spt.\rho_{l}\subseteq P_{l}
,
1\leq l\leq M
then (
258
) and (
260
) tell us that

0<2^{-m_{0}-2}\leq 2^{-k}\tau|\xi\cdot\theta|<2^{2-m_{0}},

(265)

and

|(e^{2\pi i2^{-k}\tau\theta\cdot\xi}-1)^{L}|>0.

(266)

Hence if we let

\lambda_{l,\tau\theta}(\xi):=\frac{\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}}\xi)\rho_{l}(2^{m_{0}}\xi)}{(e^{2\pi i\tau\theta\cdot\xi}-1)^{L}},

(267)

then
\lambda_{l,\tau\theta}(2^{-k}\xi)
is a well-defined function in
C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
. Using formula (
19
), we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\lambda_{l,\tau\theta}(2^{-k}\xi))*[\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)](x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\lambda_{l,\tau\theta}(2^{-k}\xi))(y)\cdot\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)(x-y)|dy.

(268)

The Fourier transform of the Schwartz function

y\mapsto\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\lambda_{l,\tau\theta}(2^{-k}\xi))(y)\cdot\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)(x-y)

is supported in
\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:|\xi|\lesssim 2^{k+J-m_{0}}\}
. Since
0<r<\min\{p,q\}<1
as mentioned in Definition
2.2
, we use (
263
), observe the simple fact that both
\int_{1}^{2}\frac{d\tau}{\tau}
and
\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(C_{l})
are fixed positive finite constants, and then apply Lemma
2.11
to (
5
) and obtain

\displaystyle|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\rho_{l}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f)(x)|^{r}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0pt[1,2]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[1,2]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[1,2]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[1,2]}}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0ptC_{l}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{C_{l}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{C_{l}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{C_{l}}}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\rho_{l}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\mathcal{F}_{n}\phi(2^{m_{0}-J-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f)(x)|^{r}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0pt[1,2]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[1,2]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[1,2]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[1,2]}}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0ptC_{l}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{C_{l}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{C_{l}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{C_{l}}}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\frac{\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\rho_{l}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)}{(e^{2\pi i2^{-k}\tau\theta\cdot\xi}-1)^{L}}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot(e^{2\pi i2^{-k}\tau\theta\cdot\xi}-1)^{L}\mathcal{F}_{n}\phi(2^{m_{0}-J-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f)(x)|^{r}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0pt[1,2]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[1,2]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[1,2]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[1,2]}}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0ptC_{l}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{C_{l}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{C_{l}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{C_{l}}}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\lambda_{l,\tau\theta}(2^{-k}\xi))*[\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)](x)|^{r}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{(J+k-m_{0})n(1-r)}\int_{1}^{2}\int_{C_{l}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\lambda_{l,\tau\theta}(2^{-k}\xi))(y)|^{r}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)(x-y)|^{r}dyd\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau}.

(269)

We let
k=0
in (
265
) and pick
m_{0}
so large that conditions of Lemma
2.15
are satisfied. Applying Lemma
2.15
to the smooth function
\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}}\xi)\rho_{l}(2^{m_{0}}\xi)
whose support set is compactly contained in
P_{l}
yields that for a sufficiently large positive integer
N
, whose value will be determined later, we can find a constant
C
such that

|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}\lambda_{l,\tau\theta}(x)|\leq\frac{C}{(1+|x|)^{N}}\qquad\text{for all }x\in\mathbb{R}^{n},

(270)

and the constant
C
may depend on
\psi,\rho_{l},m_{0},L,N
but it is independent of
\tau\in[1,2]
and
\theta\in C_{l}
. Recall that
A_{k-m}
denotes the annulus
\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:2^{m}\leq 2^{k}|y|<2^{m+1}\}
for integers
k,m
. With (
270
), we can estimate the most inside integral at the end of (
5
) as follows,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\lambda_{l,\tau\theta}(2^{-k}\xi))(y)|^{r}\cdot|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)(x-y)|^{r}dy

\displaystyle=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{A_{k-m}}2^{knr}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}\lambda_{l,\tau\theta}(2^{k}y)|^{r}\cdot|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)(x-y)|^{r}dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{m<0}2^{knr}\int_{A_{k-m}}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)(x-y)|^{r}dy

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{m\geq 0}2^{knr-mNr}\int_{A_{k-m}}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)(x-y)|^{r}dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{m<0}2^{kn(r-1)+mn}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0ptA_{k-m}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A_{k-m}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A_{k-m}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A_{k-m}}}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)(x-y)|^{r}dy

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{m\geq 0}2^{kn(r-1)+m(n-Nr)}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0ptA_{k-m}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A_{k-m}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A_{k-m}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A_{k-m}}}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)(x-y)|^{r}dy.

(271)

We insert (
5
) into (
5
), apply Fubini’s Theorem to switch the order of integration, use the following simple estimate

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0ptA_{k-m}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A_{k-m}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A_{k-m}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A_{k-m}}}\int_{1}^{2}\int_{C_{l}}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)(x-y)|^{r}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau}dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0pt|y|\leq 2^{m+1-k}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{|y|\leq 2^{m+1-k}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{|y|\leq 2^{m+1-k}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{|y|\leq 2^{m+1-k}}}\int_{1}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)(x-y)|^{r}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau}dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{n}(\int_{1}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)|^{r}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau})(x),

(272)

and also pick
N
so that
n-Nr<0
, then we obtain the estimate

\displaystyle|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\rho_{l}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f)(x)|^{r}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{(J-m_{0})n(1-r)}(\sum_{m<0}2^{mn}+\sum_{m\geq 0}2^{m(n-Nr)})

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\mathcal{M}_{n}(\int_{1}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)|^{r}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau})(x)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{(J-m_{0})n(1-r)}\mathcal{M}_{n}(\int_{1}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)|^{r}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau})(x),

(273)

where
\mathcal{M}_{n}
is the Hardy-Littlewood maximal function, and we can obtain all these inequalities above because the constant
C
in (
270
) does not rely on
\tau\in[1,2]
and
\theta\in C_{l}\subseteq\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
. Recall (
262
) and the fact that
0<r<\min\{p,q\}<1
, then we have

\displaystyle|\psi_{2^{m_{0}-k}}*f(x)|

\displaystyle=|\sum_{l=1}^{M}\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\rho_{l}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f)(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{l=1}^{M}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\rho_{l}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f)(x)|^{r})^{\frac{1}{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{(J-m_{0})n(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\mathcal{M}_{n}(\int_{1}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)|^{r}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau})(x)^{\frac{1}{r}}.

(274)

We insert (
5
) into
\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
below, incorporate those coefficients that contain
m_{0}
into constants since
m_{0}
will be fixed, apply Lemma
2.12
and then we can obtain

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle=2^{-sm_{0}}\|\{2^{ks}|\psi_{2^{m_{0}-k}}*f|\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\|\{2^{ks}(\int_{1}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)(\cdot)|^{r}

\displaystyle\quad d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau})^{\frac{1}{r}}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}.

(275)

We use Hölder’s inequality to obtain

\displaystyle(\int_{1}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)(\cdot)|^{r}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau})^{\frac{1}{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\int_{1}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)(\cdot)|^{q}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(276)

Inserting (
5
) into (
5
) yields
\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
can be estimated from above by

2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\|\{2^{ks}(\!\int_{1}^{2}\!\!\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\!|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)(\cdot)|^{q}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau})^{\frac{1}{q}}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}.

(277)

Recall (
264
) and the notation
f_{j}=\psi_{2^{-j}}*f
then we have

\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f=f-\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}f_{k+j-m_{0}}\qquad\text{in the sense of }\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n}).

(278)

We can use an argument like the one for deducing (
21
) to obtain

\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)=\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}f-\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}f_{k+j-m_{0}}\qquad\text{in the sense of }\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n}).

(279)

Inferring from (
278
) and assuming the validity of decomposition for now, then (
277
) can be estimated from above by the sum of the following two terms,

2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\|\{2^{ks}(\int_{1}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}f(\cdot)|^{q}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau})^{\frac{1}{q}}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})},

(280)

and

2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\|\{2^{ks}(\int_{1}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}f_{k+j-m_{0}})(\cdot)|^{q}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau})^{\frac{1}{q}}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}.

(281)

For the first term, we use the change of variable formulas
t=2^{-k}\tau
for
\tau\in[1,2]
and
h=t\theta
for
\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}
in a sequence and we can get

\int_{1}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}f(x)|^{q}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau}=\int_{A_{k}}\frac{|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f(x)|^{q}}{|h|^{n}}dh,

(282)

where
A_{k}
is the annulus
\{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:2^{-k}\leq|h|<2^{1-k}\}
, and hence

(\ref{eq437})\lesssim 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\cdot\|(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f(\cdot)|^{q}}{|h|^{n+sq}}dh)^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}<\infty,

(283)

and the value of the large positive integer
J
will be determined later. For the second term (
281
), we begin with the same change of variable as in (
282
) and obtain

\displaystyle\int_{1}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}(\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}f_{k+j-m_{0}}(x)|)^{q}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{kn}\int_{A_{k}}(\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{k+j-m_{0}}(x)|)^{q}dh

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}2^{kn}\int_{A_{k}}|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{k+j-m_{0}}(x)|^{q}dh

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}2^{(j-m_{0})n(\frac{q}{r}-1)}\mathcal{M}_{n}(|f_{k+j-m_{0}}|^{r})(x)^{\frac{q}{r}},

(284)

where in the penultimate expression of (
5
) we used the condition
0<q<1
, and in the last expression of (
5
) we used estimate (
5
) since
k+j-m_{0}>k+J-m_{0}>k
. Therefore we have the estimate

\displaystyle 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\|\{2^{ks}(\int_{1}^{2}\!\!\!\!\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\!\!(\!\!\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}\!\!|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}f_{k+j-m_{0}}(\cdot)|)^{q}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau})^{\frac{1}{q}}\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\|(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}2^{ksq}\cdot 2^{jqn(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{q})}\mathcal{M}_{n}(|f_{k+j-m_{0}}|^{r})(\cdot)^{\frac{q}{r}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\|(\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}2^{jq[n(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{q})-s]}\cdot\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}\mathcal{M}_{n}(|f_{k}|^{r})(\cdot)^{\frac{q}{r}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{J[n(\frac{1}{r}-1)+n(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{q})-s]}\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

(285)

where in the above calculation we incorporate coefficients containing
m_{0}
into constants since
m_{0}
is fixed. Recall the conditions
0<q<1
,
\sigma_{pq}+\tilde{\sigma}_{pq}<s
and
0<r<\min\{p,q\}
. If
\min\{p,q\}=q
, then the condition
\sigma_{pq}+\tilde{\sigma}_{pq}<s
means
s>n(\frac{1}{q}-1)
and we can pick
r
sufficiently close to
q
so that

s>n(\frac{1}{r}-1)+n(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{q})>n(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{q}).

(286)

If
\min\{p,q\}=p
, then the condition
\sigma_{pq}+\tilde{\sigma}_{pq}<s
means
s>n(\frac{1}{p}-1)+n(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{q})
and we can pick
r
sufficiently close to
p
so that (
286
) still holds true. Hence by invoking Lemma
2.12
, the last inequality (
5
) is justified. We also infer from the assumption
f\in\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and inequality (
5
) that
\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}f_{k+j-m_{0}}(x)|<\infty
for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, almost every
\tau\in[1,2],\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1},x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Therefore (
279
), (
283
), the above inference and the supposition of
f
being a function validate the decomposition

\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)(x)=\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}f(x)-\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}f_{k+j-m_{0}}(x)

(287)

in the sense of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, almost every
\tau\in[1,2],\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1},x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
when
0<p<\infty
,
0<q<1
and
\sigma_{pq}+\tilde{\sigma}_{pq}<s<\infty
, furthermore estimating (
277
) from above by the sum of (
280
) and (
281
) is justified, moreover (
281
) can be estimated from above by the beginning expression of (
5
) and hence by the ending expression of (
5
). We have reached the conclusion

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\cdot\|(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f(\cdot)|^{q}}{|h|^{n+sq}}dh)^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\quad+C^{\prime}2^{J[n(\frac{1}{r}-1)+n(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{q})-s]}\cdot\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

(288)

where the constant
C^{\prime}
is independent of
J
. From (
286
) we see that if we pick
J
sufficiently large so that the coefficient
C^{\prime}2^{J[n(\frac{1}{r}-1)+n(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{q})-s]}
is less than
\frac{1}{2}
and then shift the term
C^{\prime}2^{J[n(\frac{1}{r}-1)+n(\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{q})-s]}\cdot\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
to the left side of (
5
), then we can finish the proof of the first part of Theorem
1.6
(ii).

Next we prove the second part of Theorem
1.6
(ii) when
f
is a function,
0<p<\infty
,
1\leq q<\infty
and
-n<s<\infty
. It seems that the same method as in the proof of the first part of Theorem
1.6
(ii) produces a worse result in the case
0<p<1\leq q<\infty
, therefore we use a different method to prove the second part. Still, we assume the right side of (
37
) is finite. We use equalities (
14
) and (
15
) and integrate

[(-1)^{L+1}\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}z}f(x)]
against a Schwartz function
g(z)
of chosen properties. We let
g
be a radial Schwartz function whose radial Fourier transform
\mathcal{F}_{n}g
satisfies

0\leq\mathcal{F}_{n}g\leq 1,\quad\mathcal{F}_{n}g\text{ is supported in }\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\frac{1}{4}\leq|\xi|<4L\}

(289)

and

\mathcal{F}_{n}g(\xi)=1\text{ on }\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\frac{1}{2}\leq|\xi|<2L\}.

(290)

Since
\mathcal{F}_{n}g(0)=0
, then
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}g(z)dz=0
and we obtain the equality

\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\!\!\!\!g(z)[(-1)^{L+1}(\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}z}f)(x)]dz=\!\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}\!\!\!\!g(z)[\sum^{L}_{j=1}d_{j}f(x+2^{-k}jz)]dz=\!\sum^{L}_{j=1}d_{j}g_{2^{-k}j}*f(x),

(291)

where the kernel
G_{k}(z):=\sum^{L}_{j=1}d_{j}g_{2^{-k}j}(z)
satisfies

spt.\mathcal{F}_{n}G_{k}\subseteq\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:2^{k-2}/L\leq|\xi|\leq 2^{k+2}L\},\text{ }\mathcal{F}_{n}G_{k}(\xi)=1\text{ if }2^{k-1}\leq|\xi|<2^{k+1}.

(292)

We first estimate the term
\|\{2^{ks}G_{k}*f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}
. Since
g(z)
is a bounded Schwartz function, then
|g(z)|\lesssim|z|^{-N^{\prime}}
for
0\neq z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
N^{\prime}
can be a sufficiently large positive integer whose value will be determined later. Recall that
A_{l}=\{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:2^{-l}\leq|z|<2^{1-l}\}
and
A_{0}=\{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:1\leq|h|<2\}
and by (
291
), we have for every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

\displaystyle 2^{ks}|(G_{k}*f)(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{ks}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|g(z)|\cdot|(\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}z}f)(x)|dz

\displaystyle=2^{ks}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{A_{l}}|g(z)|\cdot|(\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}z}f)(x)|dz

\displaystyle=\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ks-ln}\int_{A_{0}}|g(2^{-l}h)|\cdot|(\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k-l}h}f)(x)|dh

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l\geq 0}2^{ks-ln}\int_{A_{0}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k-l}h}f)(x)|dh

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{l<0}2^{ks+l(N^{\prime}-n)}\int_{A_{0}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k-l}h}f)(x)|dh.

(293)

Applying Minkowski’s inequality for
\|\cdot\|_{l^{q}}
-norm for
1\leq q<\infty
to the above inequality yields

\displaystyle\|\{2^{ks}(G_{k}*f)(x)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l\geq 0}2^{-l(n+s)}(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{(k+l)sq}(\int_{A_{0}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k-l}h}f)(x)|dh)^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{l<0}2^{l(N^{\prime}-n-s)}(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{(k+l)sq}(\int_{A_{0}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k-l}h}f)(x)|dh)^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}(\int_{A_{0}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}h}f)(x)|dh)^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}},

(294)

if
N^{\prime}
is chosen so that
N^{\prime}>n+s>0
. Using Hölder’s inequality for
1\leq q<\infty
, we have

(\int_{A_{0}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}h}f)(x)|dh)^{q}\lesssim\int_{A_{0}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}h}f)(x)|^{q}dh.

(295)

Inserting (
295
) into (
5
) and applying the appropriate change of variable
z=2^{-k}h
and then inserting the resulting inequality into
\|\cdot\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
quasinorm yield

\displaystyle\|\{2^{ks}G_{k}*f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq+n)}\int_{A_{k}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{z}f)(\cdot)|^{q}dz)^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|z|^{-sq}\cdot|(\varDelta^{L}_{z}f)(\cdot)|^{q}\frac{dz}{|z|^{n}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(296)

From the first line of (
5
) and the inequality (
5
), we also deduce for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, the integral on the left end of (
291
) is absolutely convergent and hence well-defined. Thus for each
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

f_{k}(x)=\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f)(x)=\psi_{2^{-k}}*G_{k}*f(x)

(297)

and we argue as in Remark
2.5
to obtain

\displaystyle|\psi_{2^{-k}}*G_{k}*f(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|\psi_{2^{-k}}*G_{k}*f(x-z)|}{(1+2^{k+2}L|z|)^{n/r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\psi_{2^{-k}}(y)|(1+2^{k+2}L|y|)^{n/r}\cdot\frac{|G_{k}*f(x-z-y)|}{(1+2^{k+2}L|z+y|)^{n/r}}dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{n}(G_{k}*f)(x)\cdot\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|\psi_{2^{-k}}(y)|(1+2^{k+2}L|y|)^{n/r}dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{n}(G_{k}*f)(x),

(298)

and we recall that
\psi_{2^{-k}}(y)=2^{kn}\psi(2^{k}y)
thus the constant in (
5
) is independent of
k\in\mathbb{Z}
. From (
297
), (
5
) and (
2.13
) of Remark
2.13
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle=(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}|f_{k}(x)|^{q})^{p/q}dx)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}|\mathcal{P}_{n}(G_{k}*f)(x)|^{q})^{p/q}dx)^{1/p}

\displaystyle\sim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}|(G_{k}*f)(x)|^{q})^{p/q}dx)^{1/p}.

(299)

Combining (
5
) and (
5
), we conclude the proof of the second part of Theorem
1.6
(ii).

For the case
q=\infty
, we first prove Theorem
1.6
(iii). We begin with estimating the term
\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in A_{k}}2^{ks}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|
from above by the following

\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{j\leq k}2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in A_{k}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|+\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{j>k}2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in A_{k}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|.

(300)

We pick
0<\varepsilon<\min\{s,L-s\}
and estimate the first term of (
300
) as follows

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{j\leq k}2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in A_{k}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|

\displaystyle=\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{j\leq k}2^{j\varepsilon}2^{-j\varepsilon+ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in A_{k}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{j\leq k}2^{j\varepsilon}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\leq k}2^{-l\varepsilon+ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in A_{k}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{l})(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\leq k}2^{-l\varepsilon+k(s+\varepsilon)}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in A_{k}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{l})(x)|.

(301)

We use estimate (
239
) and Remark
2.7
to get

\displaystyle(\ref{eq311})

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\geq l}2^{k(s+\varepsilon-L)}2^{l(L-\varepsilon)}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{l}(x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ls}\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{l}(x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ls}\mathcal{M}_{n}(|f_{l}|^{r})(x)^{\frac{1}{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{lsr}|f_{l}|^{r})(x)^{\frac{1}{r}}.

(302)

We estimate the second term of (
300
) from above by

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{j>k}2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in A_{k}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|

\displaystyle=\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{j>k}2^{-j\varepsilon}\cdot 2^{j\varepsilon+ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in A_{k}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{j>k}2^{-j\varepsilon}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l>k}2^{l\varepsilon+ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in A_{k}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{l})(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k<l}2^{k(s-\varepsilon)}2^{l\varepsilon}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in A_{k}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{l})(x)|.

(303)

From Lebesgue’s differentiation theorem, we have
|f_{l}(x)|\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{n}(|f_{l}|^{r})(x)^{\frac{1}{r}}
for almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Putting (
241
) back into (
240
) yields the estimate

|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{l})(x)|\lesssim 2^{(l-k)n/r}\mathcal{M}_{n}(|f_{l}|^{r})(x)^{1/r}\quad\text{for }|h|\lesssim 2^{-k},l>k,

(304)

where the constant in (
304
) is independent of
h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
l,k\in\mathbb{Z}
. The value of
r\in(0,p)
will be determined later. Inserting (
304
) into (
5
) yields

(\ref{eq313})\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k<l}2^{k(s-\varepsilon-\frac{n}{r})}2^{l(\varepsilon+\frac{n}{r})}\mathcal{M}_{n}(|f_{l}|^{r})(x)^{\frac{1}{r}}.

(305)

Since
\frac{n}{s}<p
and
s-\varepsilon-\frac{n}{r}\rightarrow s-\frac{n}{p}>0
as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
and
r\rightarrow p
, we can pick
\varepsilon
sufficiently small and
r
sufficiently close to
p
so that
s-\varepsilon-\frac{n}{r}
is a positive number and hence

(\ref{eq315})\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ls}\mathcal{M}_{n}(|f_{l}|^{r})(x)^{\frac{1}{r}}\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{lsr}|f_{l}|^{r})(x)^{\frac{1}{r}}.

(306)

From the above discussion and the
L^{\frac{p}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-boundedness of the Hardy-Littlewood maximal function, we have proven that

\displaystyle\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in A_{k}}2^{ks}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(\cdot)|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
