\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{lsr}|f_{l}|^{r})(\cdot)^{\frac{1}{r}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

(307)

for all
0<p<\infty
and
\frac{n}{p}<s<L
. The above inequality and the assumption
f\in\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
have shown
\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(x)|<\infty
for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, almost every
h\in A_{k},x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. In conjunction with (
20
), we have justified the claim that

\varDelta^{L}_{h}f=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j}(x)\text{ in the sense of }\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})/\mathscr{P}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

(308)

for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, almost every
h\in A_{k},x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and hence also the inequality

\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f|}{|h|^{s}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in A_{k}}2^{ks}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j})(\cdot)|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(309)

Now (
309
) and (
5
) conclude the proof of Theorem
1.6
(iii).

To prove Theorem
1.6
(iv), we assume the right side of (
39
) is finite and use the inequality (
5
) with
N^{\prime}>n+s>0
, and then we can deduce that

\displaystyle\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ks}|G_{k}*f(\cdot)|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{l\geq 0}2^{-l(n+s)}+\sum_{l<0}2^{l(N^{\prime}-n-s)})

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ks}\int_{A_{0}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}h}f)(\cdot)|dh\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in A_{k}}2^{ks}|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f)(\cdot)|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}\frac{|(\varDelta^{L}_{h}f)(\cdot)|}{|h|^{s}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(310)

And the above estimate, in conjunction with the first line of (
5
), shows the absolute convergence of the integral on the left end of (
291
) for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Using equality (
297
), estimate (
5
), Remark
2.7
and the
L^{\frac{p}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
-boundedness of Hardy-Littlewood maximal function in a sequence, we can also obtain

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle=\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}|2^{ks}f_{k}(\cdot)|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ks}\mathcal{M}_{n}(|G_{k}*f|^{r})(\cdot)^{\frac{1}{r}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksr}|G_{k}*f|^{r})(\cdot)^{\frac{1}{r}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ks}|G_{k}*f(\cdot)|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(311)

Inequalities (
5
) and (
5
) finish the proof of Theorem
1.6
(iv). The proof of Theorem
1.6
is now complete.
∎

6.
Triebel-Lizorkin space and iterated differences along coordinate axes

Proof Of Theorem
1.9
.

We first prove inequality (
57
) when
0<p,q<\infty
,
\tilde{\sigma}^{1}_{pq}<s<L
and
f
is a tempered distribution in
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Without loss of generality, we only need to prove the inequality for
j=1
, the cases for
j=2,\cdots,n
can be proved in the same way. Recall that for
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
,
x=(x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime})
and
x_{1}^{\prime}=(x_{2},\cdots,x_{n})\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
. We still denote
f_{l}=\psi_{2^{-l}}*f
and begin with estimating
\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq+1)}\int_{2^{-k}}^{2^{1-k}}(\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)|)^{q}dt
from above by the following

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq+1)}\int_{2^{-k}}^{2^{1-k}}(\sum_{l\leq k}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)|)^{q}dt+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq+1)}\int_{2^{-k}}^{2^{1-k}}(\sum_{l>k}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)|)^{q}dt.

(312)

Using the same calculation technique exhibited in (
5
) and (
5
), if
0<\varepsilon<\min\{s,L-s\}
, we can obtain

\displaystyle(\sum_{l\leq k}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)|)^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{kq\varepsilon}\cdot\sum_{l\leq k}2^{-lq\varepsilon}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)|^{q},

(313)

\displaystyle(\sum_{l>k}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)|)^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-kq\varepsilon}\cdot\sum_{l>k}2^{lq\varepsilon}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)|^{q}.

(314)

Inserting these estimates into (
312
), we can estimate (
312
) from above by

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq+\varepsilon q+1)}\sum_{l\leq k}2^{-lq\varepsilon}\int_{2^{-k}}^{2^{1-k}}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)|^{q}dt

\displaystyle+\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq-\varepsilon q+1)}\sum_{l>k}2^{lq\varepsilon}\int_{2^{-k}}^{2^{1-k}}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)|^{q}dt.

(315)

Now we give an important estimate for
\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)
. By using Mean Value Theorem consecutively with respect to the first coordinate, we obtain

\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)=\partial^{\alpha}f_{l}(x_{1}+\lambda t,x_{1}^{\prime})\cdot t^{L}

for some
\lambda
between
0
and
L
and
\alpha=(L,0,\cdots,0)
is a multi-index. From Lemma
2.16
, we know for fixed
x_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
, the
1
-dimensional Peetre-Fefferman-Stein maximal function of
f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})
is well-defined. Using the
1
-dimensional version of Remark
2.8
, we have

|\partial^{\alpha}f_{l}(x_{1}+\lambda t,x_{1}^{\prime})|\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{1}\partial^{\alpha}f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})(x_{1}+\lambda t)\lesssim 2^{lL}\mathcal{P}_{1}f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})(x_{1}+\lambda t).

Using
1
-dimensional version of Remark
2.4
and assuming
t\lesssim 2^{-k}
, we can further obtain

\mathcal{P}_{1}f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})(x_{1}+\lambda t)\lesssim(1+2^{l-k})^{\frac{1}{r}}\mathcal{P}_{1}f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})(x_{1}),

where
0<r<\min\{p,q\}
. Therefore the estimate is given as follows

|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)|\lesssim 2^{(l-k)L}(1+2^{l-k})^{\frac{1}{r}}\mathcal{P}_{1}f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})(x_{1})\quad\text{for}\quad|t|\lesssim 2^{-k}.

(316)

For the first term in (
6
), we use the above estimate (
316
) and
1
-dimensional version of Lemma
2.6
to obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq+\varepsilon q+1)}\sum_{l\leq k}2^{-lq\varepsilon}\int_{2^{-k}}^{2^{1-k}}\!\!\!\!\!\!|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)|^{q}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}(\sum_{k\geq l}2^{kq(s+\varepsilon-L)})\cdot 2^{lq(L-\varepsilon)}\mathcal{P}_{1}f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})(x_{1})^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{lqs}\mathcal{P}_{1}f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})(x_{1})^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{lqs}\mathcal{M}_{1}(|f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r})(x_{1})^{\frac{q}{r}},

(317)

since
(1+2^{l-k})^{\frac{q}{r}}
is bounded from above by a constant when
l\leq k
, and

\mathcal{M}_{1}(|f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r})(x_{1})

is the
1
-dimensional Hardy-Littlewood maximal function of
|f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r}
centered at
x_{1}
. For the second term in (
6
), we use (
14
) to get

|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)|\lesssim\sum_{m=0}^{L}|f_{l}(x_{1}+mt,x_{1}^{\prime})|.

(318)

When
0\leq m\leq L
,
|t|\lesssim 2^{-k}
and
l>k
, we also have

|f_{l}(x_{1}+mt,x_{1}^{\prime})|\lesssim(1+2^{l-k})^{\frac{1}{r}}\mathcal{P}_{1}f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})(x_{1})\lesssim 2^{\frac{l-k}{r}}\mathcal{M}_{1}(|f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r})(x_{1})^{\frac{1}{r}},

(319)

by using
1
-dimensional versions of Remark
2.7
, Remark
2.4
and Lemma
2.6
, and constants are independent of
l,k,m,t
. And the following inequality is true for
0\leq m\leq L

\displaystyle\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0pt[2^{-k},2^{1-k}]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[2^{-k},2^{1-k}]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[2^{-k},2^{1-k}]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[2^{-k},2^{1-k}]}}|f_{l}(x_{1}+mt,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0pt|t|\leq 2^{1-k}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{|t|\leq 2^{1-k}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{|t|\leq 2^{1-k}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{|t|\leq 2^{1-k}}}|f_{l}(x_{1}+mt,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r}dt\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{1}(|f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r})(x_{1}).

(320)

Applying estimates (
318
), (
319
) and (
6
) yields the following

\displaystyle 2^{k}\int_{2^{-k}}^{2^{1-k}}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)|^{q}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{m=0}^{L}2^{k}\int_{2^{-k}}^{2^{1-k}}|f_{l}(x_{1}+mt,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r}\cdot|f_{l}(x_{1}+mt,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{q-r}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{m=0}^{L}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0pt[2^{-k},2^{1-k}]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[2^{-k},2^{1-k}]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[2^{-k},2^{1-k}]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[2^{-k},2^{1-k}]}}|f_{l}(x_{1}+mt,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r}dt\cdot 2^{(l-k)(\frac{q}{r}-1)}\mathcal{M}_{1}(|f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r})(x_{1})^{\frac{q}{r}-1}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{(l-k)(\frac{q}{r}-1)}\mathcal{M}_{1}(|f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r})(x_{1})^{\frac{q}{r}}.

(321)

And estimate (
6
) is true for
0<q<\infty
and
l>k
. Therefore we can estimate the second term in (
6
) as follows

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq-\varepsilon q+1)}\sum_{l>k}2^{lq\varepsilon}\int_{2^{-k}}^{2^{1-k}}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)|^{q}dt

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{kq(s-\varepsilon)}\sum_{l>k}2^{lq\varepsilon}\cdot 2^{(l-k)(\frac{q}{r}-1)}\mathcal{M}_{1}(|f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r})(x_{1})^{\frac{q}{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{k<l}2^{kq(s-\varepsilon-\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{q})}\cdot 2^{lq(\varepsilon+\frac{1}{r}-\frac{1}{q})}\mathcal{M}_{1}(|f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r})(x_{1})^{\frac{q}{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{lqs}\mathcal{M}_{1}(|f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r})(x_{1})^{\frac{q}{r}},

(322)

where the last step is because the assumption
\tilde{\sigma}^{1}_{pq}<s
indicates that
s-\varepsilon-\frac{1}{r}+\frac{1}{q}>0
if we pick
\varepsilon
sufficiently close to
0
and
r
sufficiently close to
\min\{p,q\}
. Combining (
312
), (
6
), (
6
) and (
6
) altogether, raising the power to
\frac{1}{q}
and inserting the result into
\|\cdot\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
quasinorm yield

\displaystyle\|(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq+1)}\int_{2^{-k}}^{2^{1-k}}(\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)|)^{q}dt)^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{lqs}\mathcal{M}_{1}(|f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r})(x_{1})^{\frac{q}{r}})^{\frac{p/r}{q/r}}dx_{1}dx_{1}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}(\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{lqs}|f_{l}(x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime})|^{q})^{\frac{p}{q}}dx_{1}dx_{1}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{p}}=\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

(323)

where we also used the
1
-dimensional version of Lemma
2.12
, and inequality (
6
) is true for
0<p,q<\infty
,
\tilde{\sigma}^{1}_{pq}<s<L
. The assumption
f\in\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and inequality (
6
) also tell the absolute convergence of the series
\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)
for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
t\in[2^{-k},2^{1-k}],x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. In conjunction with (
20
), we have proven the following claim that

\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f=\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)\text{ in the sense of }\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})/\mathscr{P}(\mathbb{R}^{n})

(324)

for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
t\in[2^{-k},2^{1-k}],x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
when
0<p,q<\infty
,
\tilde{\sigma}^{1}_{pq}<s<L
. Therefore the tempered distribution
\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f
has a function representative which is the pointwise limit of the series
\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)
and integration of
\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f
with respect to Lebesgue measure is justified. Furthermore, we have obtained the following inequality

\displaystyle\|(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-sq}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f|^{q}\frac{dt}{t})^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq+1)}\int_{2^{-k}}^{2^{1-k}}(\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)|)^{q}dt)^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

(325)

and then (
6
) and (
6
) conclude the proof of Theorem
1.9
(i).

Next, we show that inequality (
58
) is true under the conditions of Theorem
1.9
(ii). We assume the right side of (
58
) is finite, otherwise the inequality is trivial. We still use the sufficiently large positive integer
m_{0}
given in (
258
). Observe that if
\xi\in spt.\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi\subseteq A^{\prime}=\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\frac{1}{2}\leq|\xi|<2\}
, then

\frac{\xi_{1}^{2}+\xi_{2}^{2}+\cdots+\xi_{n}^{2}}{n}\geq\frac{1}{4n}.

This means given a sufficiently small positive number
\delta
, there exists at least one
\xi_{j}
such that
\delta\leq|\xi_{j}|<2
. Therefore we obtain the decomposition
A^{\prime}=\bigcup_{j=1}^{n}A^{\prime}_{j}
, where
A^{\prime}_{j}=\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\frac{1}{2}\leq|\xi|<2,\delta\leq|\xi_{j}|<2\}
. Let
\{\rho_{j}\}_{j=1}^{n}
be the partition of unity associated with this decomposition, that is, each
\rho_{j}
is a smooth function with compact support in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, and
spt.\rho_{j}
is contained in a small neighborhood of
A^{\prime}_{j}
, furthermore

\sum_{j=1}^{n}\rho_{j}(\xi)=1\text{ if }\xi\in A^{\prime}.

(326)

Without loss of generality, we can assume that

\frac{1}{2}\leq|\xi|<2\text{ and }\delta\leq|\xi_{j}|<2\text{ for }\xi\in spt.\rho_{j}.

(327)

Then we have

\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}2^{-sm_{0}}\|\{2^{ks}\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\rho_{j}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f](x)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}.

(328)

Thus to prove (
58
), it is sufficient to prove

\displaystyle\|\{2^{ks}\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\rho_{j}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f](x)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}

\displaystyle\lesssim C_{0}\|(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-sq}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,j}f(\cdot)|^{q}\frac{dt}{t})^{\frac{1}{q}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}+C_{00}\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

(329)

for every
j\in\{1,2,\cdots,n\}
and
C_{00}
is a positive constant that can be arbitrarily small. We only need to prove (
6
) for
j=1
, and the cases for
j=2,\cdots,n
can be proved in the same way. Notice that both
\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)
and
\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)
are supported in a ball centered at
0
of radius
2^{k+1-m_{0}}
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
, thus using the argument of Remark
2.5
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f](x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{n}\{\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f]\}(x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{n}\{\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f]\}(x).

(330)

By Lemma
2.6
, Remark
2.7
and Lemma
2.12
, we have

\displaystyle\|\{2^{ks}\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f](x)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\{2^{ks}\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f](x)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{L^{p}(l^{q})}.

(331)

If
1<\min\{p,q\}
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, then we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f](x)

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0pt[1,2]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[1,2]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[1,2]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[1,2]}}\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\frac{\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)}{(e^{2\pi i\cdot 2^{-k}t\xi_{1}}-1)^{L}}\cdot(e^{2\pi i\cdot 2^{-k}t\xi_{1}}-1)^{L}\mathcal{F}_{n}f](x)\frac{dt}{t}

\displaystyle=\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0pt[1,2]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[1,2]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[1,2]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[1,2]}}\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\frac{\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)}{(e^{2\pi i\cdot 2^{-k}t\xi_{1}}-1)^{L}}]*\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f(x)\frac{dt}{t}.

(332)

According to the support condition of
\rho_{1}
, when
\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}}\xi)\neq 0
and
t\in[1,2]
, we have
0<2^{-m_{0}}\delta\leq t|\xi_{1}|<2^{2-m_{0}}
and thus
|(e^{2\pi i\cdot t\xi_{1}}-1)^{L}|\geq c>0
for some constant
c
independent of
t
and
\xi_{1}
. Using the same method as in Lemma
2.15
, in particular since a similar condition like (
175
) is satisfied because of the assumption on
\rho_{1}
, we can obtain

|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\frac{\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}}\xi)}{(e^{2\pi i\cdot t\xi_{1}}-1)^{L}}](y)|\lesssim\frac{1}{(1+|y|)^{N}}

(333)

for arbitrarily large positive integer
N
, and the constant is independent of
t
. We still use the notation
A_{k-l}=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:2^{l-k}\leq|y|<2^{1+l-k}\}
and hence

\displaystyle|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\frac{\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)}{(e^{2\pi i\cdot 2^{-k}t\xi_{1}}-1)^{L}}]*\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f(x)|

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\int_{A_{k-l}}2^{kn}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\frac{\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}}\xi)}{(e^{2\pi i\cdot t\xi_{1}}-1)^{L}}](2^{k}y)|\cdot|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f(x-y)|dy

1
-dimensional Hardy-Littlewood maximal function, we can estimate the first term in (
6
) from above by the following

\displaystyle\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ls}\mathcal{M}_{1}(|f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r})(x_{1})^{\frac{1}{r}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{lsr}|f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r})(x_{1})^{\frac{p}{r}}dx_{1}dx_{1}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{lsp}|f_{l}(x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime})|^{p}dx_{1}dx_{1}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{p}}=\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(355)

When
l>k
and
|t|\lesssim 2^{-k}
, we use (
318
) and (
319
) with temporarily fixed
x_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}
to obtain

\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[2^{-k},2^{1-k})}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime})|\lesssim 2^{\frac{l-k}{r}}\mathcal{M}_{1}(|f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r})(x_{1})^{\frac{1}{r}}.

(356)

Since
\frac{1}{p}<s<L
by the assumption, we can pick
\varepsilon>0
to be sufficiently close to
0
and
r
to be sufficiently close to
p
so that
s-\frac{1}{r}-\varepsilon
is a positive finite number, and then we can obtain

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ks}\sum_{l>k}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[2^{-k},2^{1-k})}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x_{1},x_{1}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(s-\frac{1}{r})}\sum_{l>k}2^{-l\varepsilon}\cdot 2^{l(\varepsilon+\frac{1}{r})}\mathcal{M}_{1}(|f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r})(x_{1})^{\frac{1}{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k<l}2^{k(s-\frac{1}{r}-\varepsilon)}\cdot 2^{l(\varepsilon+\frac{1}{r})}\mathcal{M}_{1}(|f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r})(x_{1})^{\frac{1}{r}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ls}\mathcal{M}_{1}(|f_{l}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r})(x_{1})^{\frac{1}{r}}.

(357)

Inserting (
6
) into
\|\cdot\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
quasinorm and proceeding as in (
6
), we can estimate the second term in (
6
) from above by
\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
. Therefore we have obtained the inequality

\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[2^{-k},2^{1-k})}2^{ks}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(\cdot)|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

(358)

when
0<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
\frac{1}{p}<s<L
. The assumption
f\in\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and the above inequality also show
\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)|<\infty
for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
t\in[2^{-k},2^{1-k}),x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. In conjunction with (
20
), we have reached the conclusion that

\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f=\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)

(359)

in the sense of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})/\mathscr{P}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
t\in[2^{-k},2^{1-k}),x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
when
0<p<\infty
,
q=\infty
and
\frac{1}{p}<s<L
, furthermore we also obtain

\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}\frac{|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f|}{t^{s}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[2^{-k},2^{1-k})}2^{ks}\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(\cdot)|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(360)

Inequalities (
358
) and (
360
) conclude the proof of Theorem
1.9
(iii).

Finally, we come to the proof of Theorem
1.9
(iv). We assume the right side of (
60
) is finite, otherwise the inequality is trivial. We also use the sufficiently large positive integer
m_{0}
given in (

) and let
0<r<p
. We continue using the partition of unity

associated with the set

introduced at the beginning of the proof of Theorem

(ii), and also continue assuming

and
\delta\leq|\xi_{j}|<2
for

and

2.5
, Lemma

, and the mapping property of the Hardy-Littlewood maximal function, we can obtain the estimate

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-m_{0}s}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ks}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}(\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\rho_{j}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f)(\cdot)|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ks}\mathcal{P}_{n}(\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\rho_{j}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f])(\cdot)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ks}\mathcal{M}_{n}(|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\rho_{j}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f]|^{r})(\cdot)^{\frac{1}{r}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ks}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\rho_{j}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f](\cdot)|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(361)

We now estimate each term in the sum of (
6
). It suffices to provide estimate for the term

when

, and estimates for the terms when

can be obtained in the same way. When
1<p<\infty
, we have

\displaystyle|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f](x)|

\displaystyle=\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f](x)|

\displaystyle=\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\frac{\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)}{(e^{2\pi i\cdot 2^{-k}t\xi_{1}}-1)^{L}}\cdot(e^{2\pi i\cdot 2^{-k}t\xi_{1}}-1)^{L}\mathcal{F}_{n}f](x)|

\displaystyle=\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\frac{\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)}{(e^{2\pi i\cdot 2^{-k}t\xi_{1}}-1)^{L}}]*\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f(x)|.

We use (
6
) and the following estimate

\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0ptA_{k-l}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A_{k-l}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A_{k-l}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A_{k-l}}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f(x-y)|dy\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f(\cdot)|)(x),

(363)

and then we can estimate
|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f](x)|
from above by the following

(\sum_{l\leq 0}2^{ln}+\sum_{l>0}2^{l(n-N)})\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f|)(x)\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f|)(x)

(364)

for every

if we pick

. Therefore when

,

and

, we invoke the mapping property of Hardy-Littlewood maximal function and obtain

\displaystyle\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ks}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f]|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}2^{ks}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f|)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}\frac{|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f(\cdot)|}{t^{s}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(365)

Inequality (
6
) is still true if we replace
\rho_{1},\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1},\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}
by
\rho_{j},\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,j},\varDelta^{L}_{t,j}
respectively for
j=2,\cdots,n
. Inserting these inequalities back into (
6
) proves the first part of Theorem
1.9
(iv). To prove the second part of Theorem
1.9
(iv), we use the function

satisfying conditions (

), and the same

as in (

), (

) and the inequality

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0ptA_{k-l}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A_{k-l}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A_{k-l}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A_{k-l}}}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-J-k}}*f)(x-y)|^{r}dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-J-k}}*f)|^{r})(x),

(366)

we can obtain

\displaystyle|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f](x)|^{r}

\displaystyle=\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\frac{\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)}{(e^{2\pi i\cdot 2^{-k}t\xi_{1}}-1)^{L}}]*[\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-J-k}}*f)](x)|^{r}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{Jn(1-r)}\cdot(\sum_{l\leq 0}2^{ln}+\sum_{l>0}2^{l(n-N^{\prime}r)})

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-J-k}}*f)|^{r})(x)

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{Jn(1-r)}\mathcal{M}_{n}(\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-J-k}}*f)|^{r})(x),

(367)

if we pick
N^{\prime}>\frac{n}{r}
. From this inequality and the mapping property of the Hardy-Littlewood maximal function, we deduce the estimate

\displaystyle\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ks}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f](\cdot)|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}2^{ks}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-J-k}}*f)(\cdot)|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(368)

Assuming the validity of decomposition for now, then (
6
) can be estimated from above by the sum of

\displaystyle 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}2^{ks}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f(\cdot)|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\sim 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}\frac{|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f(\cdot)|}{t^{s}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}<\infty,

(369)

and

2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}2^{ks}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}(\sum_{l=J+1}^{\infty}f_{k+l-m_{0}})(\cdot)|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(370)

To estimate (

), we use (

), (

), Lebesgue’s differentiation theorem and the fact that

to obtain for

(371)

where the constant is independent of
l,k,t
. Therefore we have

\displaystyle 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}2^{ks}\sum_{l=J+1}^{\infty}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{k+l-m_{0}}(\cdot)|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle=2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}2^{ks}\sum_{l=J+1}^{\infty}2^{-l\varepsilon}\cdot 2^{l\varepsilon}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{k+l-m_{0}}(\cdot)|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)-J\varepsilon}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{\begin{subarray}{c}l>J\\
k\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}2^{ks+l\varepsilon}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{k+l-m_{0}}(\cdot)|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)-J\varepsilon}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{\begin{subarray}{c}l>J\\
k\in\mathbb{Z}\end{subarray}}2^{l(\varepsilon+\frac{1}{r}-s)}\cdot 2^{(k+l-m_{0})s}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\mathcal{M}_{1}(|f_{k+l-m_{0}}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r})(x_{1})^{\frac{1}{r}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(372)

The assumption
\sigma_{p}+\frac{1}{p}<s<\infty
indicates

\varepsilon+\frac{1}{r}<n(\frac{1}{r}-1)+\frac{1}{r}<s

(373)

if we pick
\varepsilon>0
to be sufficiently small and

to be sufficiently close to
p
when

. Thus by the mapping property of the

) and obtain the following estimate

\displaystyle 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}2^{ks}\sum_{l=J+1}^{\infty}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{k+l-m_{0}}(\cdot)|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{J[n(\frac{1}{r}-1)+\frac{1}{r}-s]}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n-1}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\mathcal{M}_{1}(\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksr}|f_{k}(\cdot,x_{1}^{\prime})|^{r})(x_{1})^{\frac{p}{r}}dx_{1}dx_{1}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{J[n(\frac{1}{r}-1)+\frac{1}{r}-s]}\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(374)

The assumption
f\in\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
implies
\sum_{l=J+1}^{\infty}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{k+l-m_{0}}(x)|<\infty
for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
t\in[1,2],x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. From this implication, (
21
) and (
6
), we deduce

\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-J-k}}*f)(x)=\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f(x)-\sum_{l=J+1}^{\infty}\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{k+l-m_{0}}(x)

(375)

in the sense of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
t\in[1,2],x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
when
0<p\leq 1
,
q=\infty
and
\sigma_{p}+\frac{1}{p}<s<\infty
, furthermore estimating (
6
) from above by the sum of (
6
) and (
370
) is justified, moreover (
370
) can be estimated from above by the first line of (
6
). We have obtained

\displaystyle\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ks}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f](\cdot)|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}^{\prime}2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\|\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t>0}\frac{|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f(\cdot)|}{t^{s}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}+C_{1}^{\prime\prime}2^{J[n(\frac{1}{r}-1)+\frac{1}{r}-s]}\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(376)

Inequality (
6
) is still true if we replace
\rho_{1},\varDelta^{L}_{t,1},C_{1}^{\prime},C_{1}^{\prime\prime}

respectively for

. Inserting these inequalities back into (

\displaystyle\quad+C^{\prime\prime}2^{J[n(\frac{1}{r}-1)+\frac{1}{r}-s]}\|f\|_{\dot{F}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(377)

Due to (
373
), we can pick

to be sufficiently large so that the coefficient

and shift the second term on the right side of (

) to its left side to complete the proof of Theorem
1.9
(iv). Now we conclude the proof of Theorem
1.9
.

7.
Besov-Lipschitz space and iterated differences along coordinate axes

Proof Of Theorem
1.12
.

To prove Theorem
1.12
(i), it suffices to prove inequality (
69
) for
j=1
, and the other cases for
j=2,\cdots,n
can be proven in the same way. We begin with estimating the following two terms

\displaystyle(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq+1)}\int_{2^{-k}}^{2^{1-k}}\|\sum_{l\leq k}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}dt)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}\|\sum_{l\leq k}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{l}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}},

(378)

and

\displaystyle(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq+1)}\int_{2^{-k}}^{2^{1-k}}\|\sum_{l>k}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}dt)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}\|\sum_{l>k}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{l}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(379)

We pick
0<\varepsilon<\min\{s,L-s\}
. If
1\leq p\leq\infty
, we use Minkowski’s inequality for
\|\cdot\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
-norm and obtain

\displaystyle\|\sum_{l\leq k}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{l}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{l\leq k}2^{l\varepsilon}\cdot 2^{-l\varepsilon}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{l}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})})^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{l\leq k}2^{l\varepsilon})^{q}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{j\leq k}2^{-jq\varepsilon}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{j}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{kq\varepsilon}\sum_{l\leq k}2^{-lq\varepsilon}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{l}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q},

(380)

and

\displaystyle\|\sum_{l>k}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{l}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{l>k}2^{-l\varepsilon}\cdot 2^{l\varepsilon}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{l}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})})^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{l>k}2^{-l\varepsilon})^{q}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{j>k}2^{jq\varepsilon}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{j}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-kq\varepsilon}\sum_{l>k}2^{lq\varepsilon}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{l}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}.

(381)

If
0<p<1
, then we have

\displaystyle\|\sum_{l\leq k}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{l}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{l\leq k}2^{lp\varepsilon}\cdot 2^{-lp\varepsilon}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{l}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{p})^{\frac{q}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{kq\varepsilon}\sum_{l\leq k}2^{-lq\varepsilon}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{l}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q},

(382)

and

\displaystyle\|\sum_{l>k}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{l}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{l>k}2^{-lp\varepsilon}\cdot 2^{lp\varepsilon}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{l}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{p})^{\frac{q}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-kq\varepsilon}\sum_{l>k}2^{lq\varepsilon}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{l}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}.

(383)

When
t\in[1,2]
and
l\leq k
, we use estimate (
316
) with
0<r<p
, the
1
-dimensional version of Lemma
2.6
and the mapping property of the
1
-dimensional Hardy-Littlewood maximal function to obtain

\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{l}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim 2^{(l-k)L}\|f_{l}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\text{ for }0<p\leq\infty,

(384)

where the constant is independent of
t,l,k
. Inserting (
7
), (
7
) and (
384
) into (
7
) yields

(\ref{eq604})\lesssim(\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{k\geq l}2^{kq(s+\varepsilon-L)}\cdot 2^{lq(L-\varepsilon)}\|f_{l}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(385)

We can also use estimate (
318
) and proper change of variable to obtain

\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{l}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{m=0}^{L}\|f_{l}(\cdot+2^{-k}mte_{1})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|f_{l}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

(386)

where constants are independent of
t,k,l
, and (
386
) is true for all
0<p\leq\infty
. Inserting (
7
), (
7
) and (
386
) into (
7
) yields

(\ref{eq605})\lesssim(\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{k<l}2^{kq(s-\varepsilon)}\cdot 2^{lq\varepsilon}\|f_{l}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(387)

Combining (
7
), (
7
), (
385
) and (
387
), we have proven

\displaystyle(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-sq}\|\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\!|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dt}{t})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{t\in[1,2]}\|\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{l}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

(388)

if
0<p\leq\infty
,
0<q<\infty
and
0<s<L
. The assumption
f\in\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
implies
\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)|<\infty
for almost every
t\in(0,\infty)
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. In conjunction with (
20
), we have reached the conclusion that

\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f=\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}(x)

(389)

in the sense of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})/\mathscr{P}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for almost every
t\in(0,\infty)
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
when
0<p\leq\infty
,
0<q<\infty
and
0<s<L
. Hence we also have the estimate

(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-sq}\|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dt}{t})^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-sq}\|\sum_{l\in\mathbb{Z}}|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f_{l}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dt}{t})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(390)

Inequalities (
7
) and (
390
) conclude the proof of Theorem
1.12
(i).

Now we prove the first and the second parts of Theorem
1.12
(ii). We can assume the right side of (
70
) is finite and the left side of (
70
) is positive, otherwise inequality (
70
) will be trivial. In the definition of the Peetre-Fefferman-Stein maximal function, we pick the number
r
so that
0<r<p
. We also use the positive integer
m_{0}
given in (
258
) and
\{\rho_{j}\}_{j=1}^{n}
is the partition of unity given in (

) and (

). Then we have

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle=(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{(k-m_{0})sq}\|\psi_{2^{m_{0}-k}}*f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\rho_{j}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f]\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(391)

By Remark
2.5
and Remark
2.7
, we can obtain the following pointwise estimate

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\rho_{j}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f](x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{n}\{\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\rho_{j}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f]\}(x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{n}\{\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\rho_{j}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f]\}(x)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{n}(|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\rho_{j}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f]|^{r})(x)^{\frac{1}{r}},

(392)

for
j\in\{1,\cdots,n\}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Inserting (
7
) into (
7
) and invoking the mapping property of Hardy-Littlewood maximal function yield

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\sum_{j=1}^{n}\|\{2^{ks}\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\rho_{j}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f](\cdot)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})}.

(393)

We estimate the term with
j=1
and estimates for other terms with
j=2,\cdots,n
can be obtained in the same way. If

,

and
s\in\mathbb{R}
, we use (

), (

-norm and Hölder’s inequality for

in a sequence and then we have

\displaystyle\|\{2^{ks}\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f](\cdot)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|\{2^{ks}\mathcal{M}_{n}(\int_{[1,2]}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f(\cdot)|\frac{dt}{t})(\cdot)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}(\int_{[1,2]}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\frac{dt}{t})^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}\int_{[1,2]}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dt}{t})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-sq}\|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dt}{t})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(394)

Inequality (
7
) is also true if we replace

\displaystyle 2^{ksq}(\int_{[1,2]}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\frac{dt}{t})^{q}

(396)

when

,

. Then inequality (
399
) indicates the desired inequality (
70
). Next, we come to the proof of the third part of Theorem

) when

,

, we pick

and estimate the term

(400)

If furthermore

and

satisfy

(\int_{1}^{2}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-J-k}}*f)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{r}\frac{dt}{t})^{q/r}\lesssim\int_{1}^{2}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-J-k}}*f)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dt}{t}.

Hence we can estimate (
7
) from above by

2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}\int_{1}^{2}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-J-k}}*f)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dt}{t})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

From (

\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-J-k}}*f)(x)=\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f(x)-\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{k+j-m_{0}}(x)

(403)

in the sense of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Assuming the validity of decomposition for now, we can estimate (

) from above by the sum of the following two terms,

\displaystyle 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}\int_{1}^{2}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dt}{t})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\sim 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-sq}\|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dt}{t})^{\frac{1}{q}}<\infty,

(404)

and

2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}\int_{1}^{2}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}(\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}f_{k+j-m_{0}})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dt}{t})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

To estimate (

) and the condition

and begin with the following

\displaystyle 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}\int_{1}^{2}\|\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{k+j-m_{0}}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dt}{t})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle(\ref{eq634})

Combining (

. In conjunction with (

) and (

) is true not only in the sense of

,

. Furthermore estimating (

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}^{\prime}2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}(\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\!\!t^{-sq}\|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dt}{t})^{\frac{1}{q}}+C_{1}^{\prime\prime}2^{J[n(\frac{1}{r}-1)-s]}\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(411)

To estimate (

\displaystyle 2^{ksq}(\int_{1}^{2}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{r}\frac{dt}{t})^{\frac{q}{r}}

(414)

. To estimate (

(415)

and then we can insert (

\displaystyle 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}(\int_{1}^{2}\|\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{k+j-m_{0}}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{r}\frac{dt}{t})^{\frac{q}{r}})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}(\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}\|f_{k+j-m_{0}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{r})^{\frac{q}{r}})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}(\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}2^{(m_{0}-j)sq}\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{(k+j-m_{0})sq}\|f_{k+j-m_{0}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}}

(416)

The assumption
f\in\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
tells us
\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}t,1}f_{k+j-m_{0}}(x)|<\infty
for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
t\in[1,2],x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. In conjunction with (
7
) and (
403
), we have proven (
403
) is true not only in the sense of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
but also for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
t\in[1,2],x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
when
0<q<r<p\leq 1
and
\sigma_{p}<s<L
. Furthermore estimating (
7
) from above by the sum of (
411
) and (
412
) is justified, moreover (
412
) can be estimated from above by the first line of (
7
) and hence by the last line of (

). Combining (

), (

), (

), (
7
), and (

) altogether, we have obtained

\displaystyle\|\{2^{ks}\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\rho_{1}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f](\cdot)\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{p})}

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}^{\prime}2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}(\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-sq}\|\varDelta^{L}_{t,1}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dt}{t})^{\frac{1}{r}}\cdot\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{1-\frac{q}{r}}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{1}^{\prime\prime}2^{J[n(\frac{1}{r}-1)-s]}\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(417)

Inequality (
7
) is also true if we replace
\rho_{1},\varDelta^{L}_{t,1},C_{1}^{\prime},C_{1}^{\prime\prime}
by
\rho_{j},\varDelta^{L}_{t,j},C_{j}^{\prime},C_{j}^{\prime\prime}
respectively for
j=2,\cdots,n
. Inserting these inequalities into (

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\int_{0}^{\infty}\!\!\!\!t^{-sq}\|\varDelta^{L}_{t,j}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dt}{t})^{\frac{1}{r}}\cdot\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{1-\frac{q}{r}}

\displaystyle\quad+C^{\prime\prime}2^{J[n(\frac{1}{r}-1)-s]}\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(418)

The condition
\sigma_{p}<s<L

, and hence the coefficient

, finally we can reach the desired inequality (

) when

and
\sigma_{p}<s<L
. We have finished the proof for the third part of Theorem
1.12
(ii).

where the constants are independent of

8.
Besov-Lipschitz space and iterated differences

Proof Of Theorem
1.17
.

We first prove Theorem
1.17
(i). We continue using the notation
A_{k}=\{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:2^{-k}\leq|h|<2^{1-k}\}
for
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and thus
\mathbb{R}^{n}\setminus\{0\}=\bigcup_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}A_{k}
. We also pick the number
r
in the definition of the Peetre-Fefferman-Stein maximal function so that
0<r<p
. We begin with estimating

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-sq}\|\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n}})^{\frac{1}{q}}

from above by the following

\displaystyle(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq+n)}\int_{A_{k}}\|\sum_{j\leq k}|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}dh)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\quad+(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq+n)}\int_{A_{k}}\|\sum_{j>k}|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}dh)^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(441)

We can pick
0<\varepsilon<\min\{s,L-s\}
and use the same calculation method as in (
7
), (
7
), (
7
), (
7
) to obtain

\displaystyle\|\sum_{j\leq k}|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{kq\varepsilon}\sum_{j\leq k}2^{-jq\varepsilon}\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q},

(442)

\displaystyle\|\sum_{j>k}|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-kq\varepsilon}\sum_{j>k}2^{jq\varepsilon}\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}.

(443)

And (
442
), (
443
) are true for
0<p\leq\infty
,
0<q<\infty
. To estimate the first term in (
8
), we use (
239
), Remark
2.7
and the mapping property of Hardy-Littlewood maximal function for
\|\cdot\|_{L^{\frac{p}{r}}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
-norm to obtain

\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim 2^{(j-k)L}(1+2^{j-k})^{\frac{n}{r}}\|\mathcal{P}_{n}f_{j}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim 2^{(j-k)L}\|f_{j}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

(444)

for
j\leq k
,
|h|\lesssim 2^{-k}
and constants are independent of
h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
j,k\in\mathbb{Z}
. We put (
442
) and (
444
) into the first term of (
8
) then we have

\displaystyle(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq+n)}\int_{A_{k}}\|\sum_{j\leq k}|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}dh)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{j\leq k}2^{kq(s+\varepsilon-L)}\cdot 2^{jq(L-\varepsilon)}\|f_{j}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{k\geq j}2^{kq(s+\varepsilon-L)}\cdot 2^{jq(L-\varepsilon)}\|f_{j}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(445)

To estimate the second term of (
8
), we can use (
14
) and proper change of variable to obtain

\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|f_{j}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\qquad\text{ for all }j\in\mathbb{Z},

(446)

and the constant is independent of
j\in\mathbb{Z}
and
h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. We put (
443
) and (
446
) into the second term of (
8
) then we have

\displaystyle(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq+n)}\int_{A_{k}}\|\sum_{j>k}|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}dh)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{j>k}2^{kq(s-\varepsilon)}\cdot 2^{jq\varepsilon}\|f_{j}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\sum_{k<j}2^{kq(s-\varepsilon)}\cdot 2^{jq\varepsilon}\|f_{j}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q})^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(447)

Combining (
8
), (
8
) and (
8
), we have proven

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-sq}\|\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(448)

The assumption
f\in\dot{B}^{s}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
implies
\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j}(x)|<\infty
for almost every
h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. In conjunction with (
20
), we have shown

\varDelta^{L}_{h}f=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j}(x)

(449)

in the sense of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})/\mathscr{P}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
for almost every
h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
when
0<p\leq\infty
,
0<q<\infty
and
0<s<L
. Therefore we obtain

(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-sq}\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n}})^{\frac{1}{q}}\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-sq}\|\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n}})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(450)

Inequalities (
448
) and (
450
) conclude the proof of Theorem
1.17
(i).

To prove Theorem

) is finite otherwise the inequality is trivial. We recall that
spt.\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi\subseteq A^{\prime}=\{\xi\in\mathbb{R}^{n}:\frac{1}{2}\leq|\xi|<2\}

(451)

When

, we can obtain from (

(452)

For each

,

and

, we can infer from (

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\rho_{l}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f](x)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\frac{\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\rho_{l}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)}{(e^{2\pi i\cdot 2^{-k}\tau\theta\cdot\xi}-1)^{L}}\cdot(e^{2\pi i\cdot 2^{-k}\tau\theta\cdot\xi}-1)^{L}\mathcal{F}_{n}f](x)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}\lambda_{l,\tau\theta}(2^{-k}\xi)(\cdot)*(\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}f)(x).

Due to Lemma

\displaystyle|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}\lambda_{l,\tau\theta}(2^{-k}\xi)(\cdot)*(\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}f)(x)|

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}\int_{A_{k-l}}2^{kn}|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}\lambda_{l,\tau\theta}(2^{k}y)|\cdot|(\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}f)(x-y)|dy

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0pt2^{k}|y|<1}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{2^{k}|y|<1}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{2^{k}|y|<1}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{2^{k}|y|<1}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}f)(x-y)|dy+\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}2^{l(n-N)}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0ptA_{k-l}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A_{k-l}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A_{k-l}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A_{k-l}}}|(\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}f)(x-y)|dy

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}2^{l(n-N)})\mathcal{M}_{n}(|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}f|)(x)\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{n}(|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}f|)(x),

(454)

if in the last step above we pick

. Since

\displaystyle\|\psi_{2^{m_{0}-k}}*f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l=1}^{M}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0pt[1,2]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[1,2]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[1,2]}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{[1,2]}}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0ptC_{l}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{C_{l}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{C_{l}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{C_{l}}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\rho_{l}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f]\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l=1}^{M}\int_{1}^{2}\int_{C_{l}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}\lambda_{l,\tau\theta}(2^{-k}\xi)(\cdot)*(\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}f)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{1}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{A_{k}}\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n}},

(455)

for every

. Inserting (

) when

(456)

Furthermore for each

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\mathcal{F}_{n}\phi(2^{m_{0}-J-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f](x)

\displaystyle=\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}\lambda_{l,\tau\theta}(2^{-k}\xi)(\cdot)*(\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-J-k}}*f))(x).

(457)

The Fourier transform of the Schwartz function

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{(k+J-m_{0})n(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\|2^{kn}\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}\lambda_{l,\tau\theta}(2^{k}\cdot)\cdot\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-J-k}}*f)(x\!-\!\cdot)\|_{L^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

for

\mathchoice{\mathop{\vrule width=6.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.5pt\kern-8.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{\kern-3.0ptA_{k-m}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A_{k-m}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A_{k-m}}}{\mathop{\vrule width=5.0pt,height=3.0pt,depth=-2.6pt\kern-6.0pt\intop}\nolimits_{A_{k-m}}}|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)(x-y)|^{r}dy\lesssim\mathcal{M}_{n}(|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)|^{r})(x),

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\mathcal{M}_{n}(|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)|^{r})(x)^{\frac{1}{r}},

(460)

for every

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{Jnq(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\int_{1}^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}d\mathcal{H}^{n-1}(\theta)\frac{d\tau}{\tau}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{Jnq(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\int_{A_{k}}\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n}},

(461)

and (

. We insert (

\displaystyle 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}\int_{A_{k}}\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n}})^{\frac{1}{q}}

(462)

and

(463)

To estimate (

), we begin with

\displaystyle 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}\int_{A_{k}}\|\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{k+j-m_{0}}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n}})^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{k(sq+n)}\int_{A_{k}}(\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{k+j-m_{0}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{p})^{\frac{q}{p}}dh)^{\frac{1}{q}},

(464)

since
0<p<1
. When

, we use (

) and the following inequality

\displaystyle(\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{k+j-m_{0}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{p})^{\frac{q}{p}}

. The assumption

The condition

) when

Next, we prove Theorem
1.17
(iii). We pick

and begin with estimating

(470)

The assumption

shows

when

,

and

. Therefore we have

\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-s}\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-s}\|\sum_{j\in\mathbb{Z}}|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{j}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

Inequalities (

) and (

) conclude the proof of Theorem

Now we prove Theorem
1.17
(iv). We assume the right side of (
102
) is finite, otherwise the inequality is trivial. We still use the positive integer
m_{0}
satisfying (
258
), the spherical caps
\{C_{l}\}_{l=1}^{M}
constructed right after (
258
), the corresponding sets
\{P_{l}\}_{l=1}^{M}
given in (
259
), (
260
), (
261
), and the associated smooth partition of unity
\{\rho_{l}\}_{l=1}^{M}
satisfying (
262
). When
1<p\leq\infty
, we use (
452
), (
8
) and (
8
) to obtain

\displaystyle\|\psi_{2^{m_{0}-k}}*f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l=1}^{M}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{\tau\in[1,2]}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{\theta\in C_{l}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\rho_{l}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f]\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l=1}^{M}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{\tau\in[1,2]}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{\theta\in C_{l}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}\lambda_{l,\tau\theta}(2^{-k}\xi)(\cdot)*(\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}f)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l=1}^{M}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{\tau\in[1,2]}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{\theta\in C_{l}}\|\mathcal{M}_{n}(|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}f|)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{\tau\in[1,2]}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}=\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in A_{k}}\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(472)

Therefore we have the inequality

\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ks}\|\psi_{2^{m_{0}-k}}*f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-s}\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})},

(473)

and inequality (
473
) is true for
1<p\leq\infty
,
q=\infty
and
s\in\mathbb{R}
. When
0<p<1
, we also use the function
\phi
satisfying conditions (
4
), (
263
), (
264
), and
J>m_{0}
is a large positive integer whose value will be determined later. Then we can use (
456
) and (
8
) with
0<r<p<1
to obtain

\displaystyle\|\psi_{2^{m_{0}-k}}*f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l=1}^{M}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{\tau\in[1,2]}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{\theta\in C_{l}}\|\mathcal{F}^{-1}_{n}[\mathcal{F}_{n}\psi(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\rho_{l}(2^{m_{0}-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}\phi(2^{m_{0}-J-k}\xi)\mathcal{F}_{n}f]\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\sum_{l=1}^{M}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{\tau\in[1,2]}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{\theta\in C_{l}}\|\mathcal{M}_{n}(|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)|^{r})^{\frac{1}{r}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{\tau\in[1,2]}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{\theta\in\mathbb{S}^{n-1}}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}\tau\theta}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle=2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in A_{k}}\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}(\phi_{2^{m_{0}-k-J}}*f)\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(474)

Insert (
8
) into
\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
, recall (
278
) and assume the validity of decomposition for now, then we can estimate
\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
from above by the sum of the following two terms,

2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-s}\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}<\infty

(475)

and

2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in A_{k}}2^{ks}\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}(\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}f_{k+j-m_{0}})\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(476)

To estimate (
476
), we use (
446
) and begin with the following

\displaystyle 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in A_{k}}2^{ks}\|\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{k+j-m_{0}}|\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in A_{k}}2^{ks}(\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{k+j-m_{0}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{p})^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}(\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}2^{(m_{0}-j)sp}\cdot 2^{(k+j-m_{0})sp}\|f_{k+j-m_{0}}\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{p})^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{J[n(\frac{1}{r}-1)-s]}\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(477)

The assumption
f\in\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
implies
\sum_{j=J+1}^{\infty}|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f_{k+j-m_{0}}(x)|<\infty
for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
h\in A_{k},x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. In conjunction with (
21
), (
278
) and (
475
), we have shown (
467
) is true not only in the sense of
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
but also for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
h\in A_{k},x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
when
0<p<1
,
q=\infty
and
\sigma_{p}<s<\infty
. Furthermore estimating
\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}
from above by the sum of (
475
) and (
476
) is justified, moreover (
476
) can be estimated from above by the first line of (
8
) and hence by the last line of (
8
). We have obtained the inequality

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}2^{Jn(\frac{1}{r}-1)}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-s}\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f\|_{L^{p}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\quad+C^{\prime\prime}2^{J[n(\frac{1}{r}-1)-s]}\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{p,\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(478)

The condition
\sigma_{p}<s<\infty
indicates
n(\frac{1}{r}-1)-s<0
when
r
is sufficiently close to
p
. Thus the coefficient
C^{\prime\prime}2^{J[n(\frac{1}{r}-1)-s]}
is less than
\frac{1}{2}
when the positive integer
J
is sufficiently large, and then we can shift the second term on the right side of (
8
) to its left side and prove the desired inequality (
102
). The proof of Theorem
1.17
(iv) is now complete.

Finally, we come to the proof of Theorem
1.17
(v). By using a different method, some optimal conditions can be obtained in the case
p=1
. We assume the right sides of (
103
) and (
104
) are finite. If
1\leq q\leq\infty
and
-n<s<\infty
, we use the radial Schwartz function
g
satisfying (
289
), (
290
), (
291
), and the kernel
G_{k}(\cdot)=\sum^{L}_{j=1}d_{j}g_{2^{-k}j}(\cdot)
satisfying (
292
), (
297
), (
5
) with
0<r<p=1
. Then from the inequality (
5
), we deduce

\displaystyle 2^{ks}\|G_{k}*f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{l\geq 0}2^{-l(n+s)}\cdot 2^{(k+l)s}\int_{A_{0}}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k-l}h}f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}dh

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{l<0}2^{l(N^{\prime}-n-s)}\cdot 2^{(k+l)s}\int_{A_{0}}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k-l}h}f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}dh.

(479)

When
1\leq q<\infty
, we use Minkowski’s inequality for
\|\cdot\|_{l^{q}}
-norm, Hölder’s inequality and compute as in (
5
) and (
295
) with
N^{\prime}>n+s>0
then we can obtain

\displaystyle\|\{2^{ks}G_{k}*f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{1})}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ksq}\int_{A_{0}}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}h}f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}dh)^{\frac{1}{q}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-sq}\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{q}\frac{dh}{|h|^{n}})^{\frac{1}{q}}.

(480)

When
q=\infty
, we use Minkowski’s inequality for
\|\cdot\|_{l^{\infty}}
-norm and the inequality (
8
) with
N^{\prime}>n+s>0
to obtain

\displaystyle\|\{2^{ks}G_{k}*f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{\infty}(L^{1})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ks}\int_{A_{0}}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}h}f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}dh

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{ks}\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in A_{0}}\|\varDelta^{L}_{2^{-k}h}f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathop{\rm ess\,sup\,}_{h\in\mathbb{R}^{n}}|h|^{-s}\|\varDelta^{L}_{h}f\|_{L^{1}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}.

(481)

Indicated by the first line of (
5
), (
8
), (
8
), and (
8
), we know that the integral on the left end of (
291
) is absolutely convergent and hence well-defined for every
k\in\mathbb{Z}
and almost every
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. By (
297
), (
5
), Remark
2.7
and the mapping property of Hardy-Littlewood maximal function, we have
\|f\|_{\dot{B}^{s}_{1,q}(\mathbb{R}^{n})}\lesssim\|\{2^{ks}G_{k}*f\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\|_{l^{q}(L^{1})}
for
1\leq q\leq\infty
and any
s\in\mathbb{R}
. In conjunction with (
8
) and (
8
), we have proven the inequality (
104
) and the inequality (
103
) when
p=1
,
1\leq q<\infty
and
-n<s<\infty
. To prove (
103
) is true for
p=1
,
0<q<1
and
0<s<\infty
, we notice that by picking
0<r<p=1
, the method given for the proof of the second part of Theorem
1.17
(ii) still applies and
\sigma_{1}=0
. The proof of Theorem
1.17
is now complete.
